-module
V\otimes R
.

2.3
Symmetric sequences and derived pd operads.

Construction
2.2.12
produced the monad
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}
from the monad of free
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for
R
a discrete commutative ring. There exists another monad on the abelian symmetric monoidal category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
called
the free non-unital divided power algebra
monad
\Gamma^{+}_{R,\heartsuit}
whose underlying functor is

\Gamma^{+}_{R,\heartsuit}(X)=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}(X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}.

(2.6)

This monad can be defined on the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for any connective derived commutative ring by means of the theory of
derived symmetric sequences
. Let us recall the following classical definition.

Definition 2.3.1
.

Let
\mathrm{Spt}^{\mathrm{B}\Sigma_{n}}
be the
\infty
-category of representations of the symmetric group
\Sigma_{n}
in spectra. We let the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences
to be the product:

\mathrm{SSeq}:=\underset{n\geq 0}{\prod}\mathrm{Spt}^{\mathrm{B}\Sigma_{n}}.

Below we will recall the universal property of the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences which we learnt from
[
11
, Section 1.1]
.

Remark 2.3.2
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}
is the free stable presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category on the unit object
\mathrm{Spt}\in\Pr^{L}
. The tensor product of two symmetric sequences
\{P(n)\}
and
\{Q(n)\}
is given by the formula

(P\otimes Q)(n)=\bigoplus_{i+j=n}\Ind_{\Sigma_{i}\times\Sigma_{j}}^{\Sigma_{n}}P(i)\otimes Q(j).

The universal property of being free on one generator implies that there is an equivalence

\mathrm{SSeq}\simeq\Fun_{\mathrm{Cat}}(*,\mathrm{SSeq})\simeq\Fun_{\Pr^{L}}(\mathrm{Spt},\mathrm{SSeq})\simeq\End_{\CAlg(\Pr^{L})}(\mathrm{SSeq}).

(2.7)

The equivalence
2.7
gives a monoidal structure on the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}
, called the
composition product
. For two objects
\{P(n)\},\{Q(n)\}\in\mathrm{SSeq}
, there is a formula

P\circ Q\simeq\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})_{h\Sigma_{n}},

where the tensor product is taken with respect to the symmetric monoidal structure in
\mathrm{SSeq}
. An
operad (in spectra)
is an algebra object in
\mathrm{SSeq}
with respect to the composition product symmetric monoidal structure.

Construction 2.3.3
.

The forgetful functor
\CAlg(\Pr^{L})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
is represented by
\mathrm{SSeq}
. Consequently, the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}
acts on the underlying
\infty
-category of any symmetric monoidal stable presentable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{C}
. The action functor
-\circ:\mathrm{SSeq}\rightarrow\End(\mathrm{C})
is given by the formula

P\circ X=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}(P(n)\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})_{h\Sigma_{n}}.

Notice that this action preserves sifted colimits. Given an operad
\mathcal{O}\in\Alg(\mathrm{SSeq})
, we obtain a sifted colimit preserving monad
\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{O}}:=\mathcal{O}\circ-:\mathrm{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}
acting on
\mathrm{C}
.

It was observed by B.Fresse
[
10
]
and later in the
\infty
-categorical context by Francis and Gaitsgory
[
FrG
]
that there is another monoidal structure on
\mathrm{SSeq}
which acts naturally on any stable presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. The product in this monoidal structure is given by the formula

P\overline{\circ}Q=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})^{h\Sigma_{n}},

and the action on any symmetric monoidal stable presentable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{C}
is given by

P\overline{\circ}X=(\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}P\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})^{h\Sigma_{n}}.

The rigorous construction of this monoidal structure and the action on any symmetric monoidal stable presentable
\infty
-category are not essential to the current text. We would only like to remark that in the particular case of the object
(0,\mathbb{S},\mathbb{S},...)
, the monad
\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}(X^{\otimes^{n}})^{h\Sigma_{n}}
can be called the monad of
“free non-unital
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-divided power algebra”
. One crucial difference with the free
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra monad is that the free
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-divided power algebra monad does not preserve connective objects, and therefore can not be treated in the same way as in Construction
2.2.12
. Instead, we will construct a natural action of the monoidal category of discrete symmetric sequences on
\mathrm{Mod}_{P,\heartsuit}
for any polynomial ring
P
, and produce an action of
derived symmetric sequences
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for any connective derived commutative ring
R
using the formalism of derived functors. We should note that such construction is essentially contained in
[
6
]
, and our goal here is to merely review it and fix the relevant notation.

Construction 2.3.4
.

Here we recall the construction of derived symmetric sequences given in
[
6
, Notation 3.52]
. Let
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{n}}]\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma_{n}],\heartsuit}
be the full additive subcategory in the abelian category of
\Sigma_{n}
-representations spanned by
\mathbb{Z}
-linearizations of finite
\Sigma_{n}
-sets. Let
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{n}}]
, understood as direct sum of additive categories. An object of
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
consists of a
finite
collection
\{P(i)\}
of
\Sigma_{i}
-representations in
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
, i.e.
P(n)=0
for any large enough
n
. We define the
\infty
-category of
derived symmetric sequences
as the
\infty
-category of left modules over the additive category
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
, i.e. the
\infty
-category of additive functors

\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]}=\Fun^{\oplus}(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}]^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Spt}).

The additive category
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
, understood as a subcategory
\mathrm{SSeq}_{\heartsuit}\subset\mathrm{SSeq}
of the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences in spectra, is closed under tensor product and composition defined in Construction
2.7
(for explanation, see
[
6
, Construction 3.58]
). Moreover, the duality equivalence
(-)^{\vee}:\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]^{\mathrm{op}}\simeq\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
allows us to define a new monoidal structure on
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
by the formula:

P\overline{\circ}Q:=(P^{\vee}\circ Q^{\vee})^{\vee}=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}.

For any pair of objects
P,Q\in\mathbb{Z}[\Sigma]
, there is a Norm map

\Nm:(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})_{\Sigma_{n}}\rightarrow(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}},

and this induces a lax monoidal functor
(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\circ)\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\overline{\circ})
.

[
6
, Proposition 3.72]
shows that the composition product
\overline{\circ}
right-left extends to a monoidal structure on
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
, and restriction by the Norm
(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\circ)\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\overline{\circ})
extends to a right lax monoidal functor
\Nm^{*}:(\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}},\circ)\rightarrow(\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}},\overline{\circ})
which is identical on objects.

Notation 2.3.5
.

Let us fix some notation related to Construction
2.3.4
.

1.

\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{0}
is the monoidal category
(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\circ)
.

2.

\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}_{0}
is the monoidal category
(\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}],\overline{\circ})
.

3.

\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is the presentable monoidal
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}},\circ)
.

4.

\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
is the presentable monoidal
\infty
-category
(\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}},\overline{\circ})
.

Remark 2.3.6
.

To construct a compatible action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on some stable
\infty
-category, it is sufficient to construct an action of the latter
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
as the action of the former
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
can then be obtained by restricting along the lax monoidal functor
\Nm^{*}:\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
.

Following observation of
[
6
, Proposition 3.72 (4)]
, we record the following proposition. We say that a derived symmetric sequence
P\in\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
is
concentrated in arities
\geq 1
if
P(0)\simeq 0
.

Proposition 2.3.7
.

The functor
\Nm^{*}
is a
monoidal
equivalence on the full subcategories of derived symmetric sequences concentrated in arities
\geq 1
.

Proof.

Take two objects
P,Q\in R[\Sigma]
which are trivial in arity
0
5
5

5

In fact, in the subsequent argument it suffices to assume that only
Q
is trivial in arity
0
.
, and consider the symmetric sequence
P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}}
. We wish to prove that the map
\Nm_{\Sigma_{n}}:(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})_{\Sigma_{n}}\rightarrow(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}
is an equivalence. To see this, we look at the formula for the arity
r
module of the symmetric sequence
Q^{\otimes^{n}}
:

Q^{\otimes^{n}}(r)=\bigoplus_{i_{1}+...+i_{n}=r}\Ind_{\Sigma_{i_{1}}\times...\times\Sigma_{i_{n}}}^{\Sigma_{r}}Q(i_{1})\otimes...\otimes Q(i_{n}).

(2.8)

By assumption,
Q(i)=0
unless
i\geq 1
. It implies that the right hand side in Equation
2.8
is
0
unless
n\leq r
, because if
n>r
, then in order for the sum
i_{1}+...+i_{n}
to be equal
r
, at least one of the indices must be
0
, which forces the tensor product to vanish. In the case
n\geq r
, we claim that the right hand side of Equation
2.8
is an induced
\Sigma_{n}
-representation. Indeed, consider a set of indices
i_{1},...,i_{n}\geq 1
such that
i_{1}+...+i_{n}=r
. The sum in the formula
2.8
contains as a summand the
\Sigma_{n}
-submodule

\bigoplus_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{n}}\Ind_{\Sigma_{i_{\sigma(1)}}\times...\times\Sigma_{i_{\sigma(n)}}}^{\Sigma_{r}}(Q(i_{\sigma(1)})\otimes...\otimes Q(i_{\sigma(n)})),

(2.9)

where the sum is taken with respect to all permuations of the indices. The
\Sigma_{n}
-submodule in Equation
2.9
is
\Sigma_{n}
-induced. As the sum in Equation
2.8
splits into the sum of
\Sigma_{n}
- submodules of the form
2.9
for various collections
i_{1},...,i_{n}
, it follows that the right hand side of
2.8
is an induced
\Sigma_{n}
-module, and the Norm map induces an isomorphism between its
\Sigma_{n}
-orbits and
\Sigma_{n}
-fixed points, or equivalently, its Tate fixed points module vanishes. But the full subcategory of
\Sigma_{n}
-representations whose Tate fixed points vanishes, is a
\otimes
-ideal in the category of all
\Sigma_{n}
-representations. Therefore, the Tate fixed points module of
X(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}}(r)
also vanishes for
n\geq r
, and the Norm map
(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}}(r))_{\Sigma_{n}}\rightarrow(P(n)\otimes Q^{\otimes^{n}}(r))^{\Sigma_{n}}
is an equivalence, as desired.
∎

Definition 2.3.8
.

A
derived (pd) operad
is an algebra object in the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
(respectively, in the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
).

Remark 2.3.9
.

Proposition
2.3.7
implies that given a derived operad
O
concentrated in arities
\geq 1
gives rise to a pd operad. As an example, consider derived symmetric sequence
\mathcal{O}:=(0,\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z},...)
. We compute that

\mathcal{O}\circ\mathcal{O}(k)\simeq\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\bigl(\bigoplus_{i_{1}+...+i_{k}=n}\mathbb{Z}\bigr).

The map
\mathcal{O}\circ\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}
given by the natural map
\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\bigl(\bigoplus_{i_{1}+...+i_{k}=n}\mathbb{Z}\bigr)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
in every arity
k
, endows
\mathcal{O}
with the structure of a derived operad. This derived operad is the
non-unital derived commutative operad
. As it vanishes in arity
0
, there is a corresponding deried pd operad which is called
the non-unital pd commutative operad
.

Below we will construct an action of the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for a discrete commutative ring
R
.

Construction 2.3.10
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring, and consider the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and the full subcategory of finitely generated free modules inside all connective
R
-modules
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}
.

The subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
has the following properties.

- For any object
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
, the dual
X^{\vee}
is in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
again;

- For any
X\in\mathscr{\mathrm{Mod}}_{R,0}
and any finitely generated free
\Sigma_{n}
-module
V
, the object
(V\otimes X^{\otimes{n}})_{\Sigma_{n}}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
is in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
again.

Together, these two conditions imply that for any finitely generated free
\Sigma_{n}
-module
V
, the functor
X\longmapsto(V\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
preserves the full subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
.

Therefore, there is a functor

\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{n}}]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\End(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})

sending

V\longmapsto\bigl(X\longmapsto(V(n)\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}\bigr).

Note that we switched invariants and coinvariants in the formula above by using the isomorphism

(V(n)\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}\simeq\bigl((V^{\vee}(n)\otimes(X^{\vee})^{\otimes^{n}})_{\Sigma_{n}}\bigr)^{\vee}

together with two conditions listed above. Moreover, for each
n
, the functor
X\longmapsto(V\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})_{\Sigma_{n}}
is additively polynomial of degree
n
, and it follows by duality that the functor
X\longmapsto(V\otimes X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}
is additively polynomial of degree
n
again. Since each object
\{P(i)\}
in
\mathbb{Z}[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
is non-zero for only finitely many
i
, it follows
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
acts on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
by additively polynomial functors. To show that this action extends to an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, we shall use
[
6
, Proposition 3.48]
which states that the functor
\mathrm{Mod}:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
from the
\infty
-category of small additive
\infty
-categories and additive functors
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add}}
to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
of presentable stable
\infty
-categories and sifted colimit preserving functors, refines to a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Mod}:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
, where
\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}}
is the
\infty
-category of small additive
\infty
-categories and additively polynomial functors between them.

Construction 2.3.11
.

The preceeding discussion of the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for any discrete commutative ring
R
generalizes to any connective derived commutative ring. To this end, we have constructed a functor

\textstyle{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Mod}_{(-)}:\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{poly}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})}

which sends a polynomial ring
R
to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
endowed with the sifted colimit preserving action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
constructed in Construction
2.3.10
. Left Kan extending along the inclusion
\CAlg^{\mathrm{poly}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
, we obtain a sifted colimit preserving functor

\textstyle{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}\curvearrowright\mathrm{Mod}_{(-)}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})}

such that the composition with the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
is equivalent to the functor of left modules
R\longmapsto\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. The latter follows from the fact that the forgetful functor preserves sifted colimits and the functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{(-)}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
is itself left Kan extended from
\CAlg^{\mathrm{poly}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Definition 2.3.12
.

Let
R
be a connective derived ring. Using Remark
2.3.9
, Construction
2.3.11
gives rise to a monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{\mathrm{R}}\in\End^{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
whose underlying functor satisfies
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}=\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}(X^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}
for any
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}
. We refer to this as
the free non-unital divided power (pd) algebra monad
, and algebras over this monad are called
non-unital derived divided power (pd) algebras
. We denote

\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}:=\Alg_{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}^{+}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

A map
R\rightarrow S
of connective derived commutative rings gives a base change functor
-\otimes_{R}S:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{S}
, as well as restriction
\mathrm{DAlg}_{S}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{R}.

Construction 2.3.13
.

Let
X
be a derived prestack, i.e. a functor
X:\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spc}
. Construction
2.3.11
implies that the
\infty
-category
\QCoh(X)
of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
has an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
. Indeed, this follows by writing the
\infty
-category
\QCoh(X)
as the limit

\QCoh(X)=\underset{\Spec R\rightarrow X}{\lim}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

along pull-back functors for all affines
\Spec A\rightarrow X
mapping to
X
, and using the fact that the forgetful functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
preserves limits. Consequently, we can simply
define
the
\infty
-category of derived commutative (respectively, derived non-unital divided power) algebra objects in
\QCoh(X)
as the limit

\mathrm{DAlg}^{(\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd})}(\QCoh(X))\simeq\underset{\Spec R\rightarrow X}{\lim}\mathrm{DAlg}^{(\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd})}_{R}.

In some cases we have to work with a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{C}
which is not (or at least, not known to be) of the form
\QCoh(X)
for a derived prestack
X
. In such a case, one approach to constructing an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
is to specify a
t
-structure
(\mathrm{C}_{\geq 0},\mathrm{C}_{\leq 0})
and a choice of a subcategory
\mathrm{C}^{0}
of compact generators in
\mathrm{C}
satisfying certain properties which will be listed below (a derived algebraic context in the sense of
[
26
]
). For such a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{C}
, we will be able to construct a sifted colimit preserving action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{C}
. This should be compared with the construction of derived algebras in a derived algebraic context in
[
26
]
, albeit the types of
\infty
-categories we consider below are slightly different from derived algebraic contexts of Raksit.

Construction 2.3.14
.

Let
\mathrm{C}
be a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category equipped with a compatible
t
-structure
(\mathrm{C}_{\geq 0},\mathrm{C}_{\leq 0})
and a full subcategory
\mathrm{C}_{0}\subset\mathrm{C}_{\heartsuit}\subset\mathrm{C}_{\geq 0}
consisting of compact projective generators of
\mathrm{C}_{\geq 0}
. We require that the
t
-structure is left complete, and the full subcategory
\mathrm{C}_{0}
is closed in
\mathrm{C}_{\heartsuit}
under the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}_{0}
,
and
the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. Using the strategy of Construction
2.3.10
, there is a sifted colimit preserving action of the monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{C}
. Moreover, this construction is functorial in
\mathrm{C}
in the following sense. Notice that for
\mathrm{C}
as above, there is an equivalence
\mathrm{C}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{C}_{0})
. Consequently, using
[
6
, Proposition 3.48]
again as in Construction
2.3.10
, we obtain a functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}_{0}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}).

Therefore, the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\mathrm{C}
is functorial in symmetric monoidal right
t
-exact functors
F:\mathrm{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{D}
such that
F(\mathrm{C}_{0})\subset\mathrm{D}_{0}
. The same construction applies to the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
. Note that if we drop the condition that
\mathrm{C}_{0}
is closed under the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{0}
, we still get a
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
-action on
\mathrm{C}
by restricting along
\Nm^{*}:\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}\rightarrow\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
, however, the naturality of this action could be more subtle without the aforementioned condition.

Observation 2.3.15
.

In studying the
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}
-monad defined in Definition
2.3.12
in such a context as in Construction
2.3.14
, restriction
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}|_{\mathrm{C}_{0}}
to
\mathrm{C}_{0}\subset\mathrm{C}
does not yield a monad. However, as each
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}
preserves
\mathrm{C}_{0}
, it follows that
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}
preserves the full subcategory
\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})\subset\mathrm{C}
, and hence
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}|_{\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})}:\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})\rightarrow\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})
yields a monad. Moreover, the original monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}
on
\mathrm{C}
is uniquely determined by
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}|_{\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})}
in the following sense. Let
\End_{\Sigma,RL}^{\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})}(\mathrm{C})\subset\End_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{C})
be the subcategory of endofunctors preserving the full subcategory
\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})\subset\mathrm{C}
which are right-left extended from
\mathrm{C}_{0}\subset\mathrm{C}
. Then the restriction functor

\textstyle{\End^{\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0})}_{\Sigma,RL}(\mathrm{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\End_{\omega}(\Ind(\mathrm{C}_{0}))}

is an equivalence. We will use this observation for constructing maps of monads right-left extended from
\mathrm{C}_{0}
.

The same applies to the monad
\mathrm{LSym}:\mathrm{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}
, as each
\mathrm{LSym}^{n}:\mathrm{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}
preserves the full subcategory
\mathrm{C}_{0}
.

Let us finish this section with some observations on how lax symmetric monoidal functors are incorporated in this setting.

Construction 2.3.16
.

Let
\mathrm{A}_{0}
be a small additive
\infty
-category, consider the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}^{0}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}:=\Fun(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{A}_{0},\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}.

Composing with the monoidal functor
\mathrm{Mod}:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
, we obtain a functor

\Fun(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{A}_{0},\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}\rightarrow\Fun(\mathrm{B}\mathrm{A}_{0},\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})_{\mathrm{lax}}.

Notice that for any
\mathrm{C}\in\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
, the endomorphism object
\Fun_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{C},\mathrm{C})
is a presentable stable monoidal
\infty
-category. Therefore, any monoidal functor
\mathrm{A}_{0}\rightarrow\Fun_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{C},\mathrm{C})
extends to a continuous monoidal functor
\mathrm{A}:=\mathrm{Mod}(\mathrm{A}_{0})\rightarrow\Fun_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{C},\mathrm{C})
, and we obtain a functor

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}_{0}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})_{\mathrm{lax}},

where
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}_{0}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}:=\Fun(\textbf{BA},\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})_{\mathrm{lax}}
.

A consequence of this construction is the following example.

Example 2.3.17
.

Assume

I\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}_{0}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}})_{\mathrm{lax}}

is a diagram. Then applying
\mathrm{Mod}:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
, we obtain a diagram

I\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{A}}(\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma})_{\mathrm{lax}}.

We will sometimes apply this example in the setting
\mathrm{A}_{0}:=\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}_{0}
and
\mathrm{A}:=\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
.

2.4
Recollection on gradings and filtrations.

Definition 2.4.1
.

We let
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
be the discrete category whose objects are integers
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and all morphisms are identities, and
\mathbb{Z}_{\leq*}
be the nerve of the partially ordered set of integers. Note that both
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{\leq*}
are symmetric monoidal categories with respect to the operation of addition. In what follows, we will work over a connective derived commutative ring
R
.

1.

We define the
\infty
-category of
graded objects
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, denoted
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to be the
\infty
-category of functors

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}:=\Fun((\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

An object
X\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the same data as a collection of objects
\{X^{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
parametrized by all integers
n
. We will often denote a graded object
X
as
X^{\bullet}
to emphasize the grading. The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
carries a
Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure
with tensor product given by the formula

(X^{\bullet}\otimes Y^{\bullet})^{n}:=\bigoplus_{p+q=n}X^{p}\otimes Y^{q}.

(2.10)

2.

W let the
\infty
-category of
filtered objects
6
6

6

To be more precise, we are dealing with
decreasing filtrations
here.

\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to be the
\infty
-category of functors

\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}:=\Fun((\mathbb{Z}_{\leq*})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

An object
X\in\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the data of a collection of objects
\{X^{\geq n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, and maps
X^{\geq m}\rightarrow X^{\geq n}
for any pair of integers
m
and
n
such that
m\geq n
. We can depict an object
X\in\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
as a diagram

\{...\rightarrow X^{\geq 2}\rightarrow X^{\geq 1}\rightarrow X^{\geq 0}\rightarrow X^{\geq-1}\rightarrow X^{\geq-2}\rightarrow...\}

in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. We will often denote the data of a filtered object by the symbol
X^{\geq\star}
to emphasize the filtration. The
\infty
-category
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has a
Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure
with tensor product given by the formula

(X^{\geq\star}\otimes Y^{\geq\star})^{n}:=\colim_{p+q\geq n}X^{\geq p}\otimes Y^{\geq q}.

(2.11)

3.

The
associated graded object
functor
\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the functor sending a filtered object
X^{\geq\star}\in\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to a graded object
\gr^{\bullet}(X)
with the
n
-th graded piece defined by the formula

\gr(X)^{n}:=X^{n}/X^{n+1}=\cofib(X^{n}\rightarrow X^{n+1}).

By
[
18
, Proposition 3.2.1]
, this functor is symmetric monoidal (alternatively, see Remark
2.4.3
).

4.

We define a
forgetful functor

(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}):\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which sends a filtered object
X:=X^{\geq\star}
to the underlying graded object whose weighted pieces are
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})(X)^{n}:=X^{\geq n}.
This functor is right lax symmetric monoidal (see Remark
2.4.3
for explanation)

5.

We let
\mathrm{ins}^{n}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the
insertion functor
sending an object
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to the graded object
\mathrm{ins}^{n}(X)^{\bullet}
which has
\mathrm{ins}^{n}(X)^{i}=X
when
i=n
, and
\mathrm{ins}^{n}(X)^{i}=0
if
i\neq 0
. It has a right adjoint
evaluation functor

\ev^{n}:\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
sending a graded object
X^{\bullet}
to its
n
-th graded piece
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. For
n=0
, the functor
\mathrm{ins}^{0}
is symmatric monoidal, and the functor
\ev^{0}
is
right-lax symmetric monoidal
.

6.

Similarly, we let
\mathrm{ins}^{n}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the insertion functor sending an
R
-module
M
to the filtered object which has
M
in degree
n
, and is
0
otherwise. It has a left adjoint
\gr^{n}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and a right adjoint
\ev^{n}
. The functor
\mathrm{ins}^{0}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is symmetric monoidal, and its right adjoint
\ev^{0}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is lax symmetric monoidal.

7.

Let
X=X^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. We define a new graded object
X(i)
, called the
i
-th twist
of
X
, by the formula
X(i)^{n}=X^{n-i}
. For
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, we will also often denote
X(i):=\mathrm{ins}^{i}(X)
.

8.

There exists a
constant filtration
functor
\const:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which sends any
R
-module
M
to the filtered object

\textstyle{\const(M):=\{...\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{...\}.}

It has a left adjoint functor sending a filtered object
X^{\geq\star}
to the colimit
\underset{\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq*}}{\colim}\>X^{\geq\star}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. We will often use the notation
|X|=\underset{\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leq*}}{\colim}\>X^{\geq\star}
.

9.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
contains a full symmetric monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\subset\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
consisting of
non-negatively graded objects
, i.e. graded objects
X^{\bullet}
with
X^{n}=0
for
n<0
.

10.

A filtered object
X^{\geq\star}
is
non-negatively filtered
if all maps
X^{n}\rightarrow X^{n-1}
are equivalences for
n\leq 0
. There is a symmetric monoidal subcategory
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\subset\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
spanned by
non-negatively filtered objects
in all filtered objects.

In the next sections, we will mainly work with non-negative filtrations/gradings. Therefore, by a slight abuse of notation, we will often speak of non-negatively filtered/graded objects as simply filtered/graded objects.

Construction 2.4.2
.

Let us work in the case
R=\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\mathbb{Z}[t]
be the free commutative ring on one generator, and assign the element
t
degree
-1
. By
[
18
, Proposition 3.1.6]
, there is a symmetric monoidal
Rees equivalence
(or
Rees construction
),

\mathrm{Rees}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.875pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}).

(2.12)

This equivalence sends a filtered object
X^{\geq\star}
to the underlying graded object with a graded action of the graded algebra
\mathbb{Z}[t]
induced by the filtration.

The perspective of the Rees construction is closely related with the stacky approach to filtrations. Let
\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the multiplicative group scheme, and
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}
the classifying stack of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Then there is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories (see
[
23
, Theorem 4.1]
)

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\QCoh(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}).

Also, let
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the quotient of affine line by the standard
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. There is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories:

\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}[t]}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}).

(2.13)

Combining equivalences
2.12
and
2.13
, we obtain an equivalence

\textstyle{\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})}

giving a description of the
\infty
-category of filtered objects as quasi-coherent sheaves on a stack.

For a connective derived commutative algebra
R
, we have

\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\underset{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\Spec R).

It follows from Construction
2.3.13
that
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
supports the notion of derived commutative (pd) algebras therein.

Remark 2.4.3
.

From the point of view of Construction
2.4.2
, the functor
\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the pullback of quasi-coherent sheaves along the inclusion of the
0
-section
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
. The functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}):\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
can be interpreted as the push-forward of quasi-coherent sheaves along the map
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}.
It follows from this interpretation that
\gr
is symmetric monoidal, and
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})
is right lax symmetric monoidal.

Construction 2.4.4
.

For a general connective derived commutative ring
R
, we can construct several different
t
-structures on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. We will record the construction of two of them.

1.

The
neutral

t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has connective part
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}
spanned by graded objects
X^{\bullet}
for which
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}
for all
n
. The coconnective part of the neutral
t
-structure consists of objects
X^{\bullet}
for which
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\leq 0}
for all
n
. The neutral
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has the heart
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
consisting of graded objects in the abelian category of
t
-discrete objects in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

2.

Another important
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the
negative

t
-structure. It has the connective part
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0^{-}}
spanned by graded objects
X^{\bullet}
which have
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq-n}
for any
n
, and the coconnective part spanned by objects with
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\leq-n}
for any
n
. The heart of this
t
-structure is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of graded objects in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
again, albeit in a non-tautological way: a typical
t
-discrete object
X^{\bullet}
in the negative
t
-structure has each
X^{n}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R},[-n,-n],
which is a discrete
R
-module concentrated in degree
-n
.

Remark 2.4.5
.

The negative
t
-structure is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure. Indeed, given two objects
X^{\bullet},Y^{\bullet}\in\mathscr{\mathrm{Gr}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0^{-}}
, we have
X^{p}\otimes Y^{q}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\leq-p-q}
since
X^{p}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\leq-p}
and
Y^{q}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\leq-q}
, and the formula
2.10
implies that
X^{\bullet}\otimes Y^{\bullet}
is connective in the negative
t
-structure. In particular, the heart
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit^{-}}
becomes a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.

Recall that to define a discrete additive symmetric monoidal category
\mathrm{C}
, we need to give a bilinear functor
-\otimes-:\mathrm{C}\times\mathrm{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{C}
, and a natural
\Sigma_{2}
-action on the tensor product
X\otimes Y
of any pair of elements (a
“braiding”
), which is subject to the constraints listed, for instance, in
[
17
, Chapter XI, 1]
. The derived
\infty
-category
\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{C}):=\Fun^{\oplus}(\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Spt})
then acquires a symmetric monoidal structure via right-left extension.

Construction 2.4.6
.

In this construction, we will define the so-called
Koszul sign rule
symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. First, we define a tensor product on the abelian category
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
such that for two objects
X,Y\in\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, the tensor product of
X
and
Y
coincides with the usual tensor product
X\otimes Y
of graded modules, but the action of
\Sigma_{2}
is twisted by the sign permutation, i.e. for an element
x\otimes y\in X\otimes Y
, the generator
\sigma\in\Sigma_{2}
acts by
\sigma(x\otimes y)=-y\otimes x
. This defines a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
which restricts to a symmetric monoidal structure on the additive category
\Proj^{\omega}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit})
of compact projective objects in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
, and right-left extends to a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

It turns out that the heart of the negative
t
-structure is equivalent to
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
with Koszul sign rule symmetric monoidal structure via the following construction.

Construction 2.4.7
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
carries a
shear
auto-equivalence
\textstyle{[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
defined by the formula
(X[\bullet])^{i}=X^{i}[i]
for any graded object
X:=X^{\bullet}
.

Below will establish the main properties of the shear equivalence. These statements are well-known. For instance, Proposition
2.4.9
is essentially an equivalent way of formulating property 2) of Proposition
2.4.8
below, and it was already established by L.Illusie in the text
[
15
]
.

Proposition 2.4.8
.

The shear construction has the following properties:

1.

It is left and right
t
-exact with respect to the standard
t
-structure on the target, and the
negative

t
-structure on the source.

2.

It intertwines the
Koszul sign rule
symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
with the standard symmetric monoidal structure. In particular, it induces an equivalence of abelian symmetric monoidal categories
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit^{-}}
, where the left hand side is endowed with the Koszul sign rule tensor product.

Proof.

Identification of
t
-structures follows immediately from definition of the negative
t
-structure and the formula for the shear equivalence.

Since both symmetric monoidal structures are right-left extended the symmetric monoidal structures on the hearts, it suffices to show the statement in the following setting. Given two objects
X,Y\in\Proj^{\omega}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit})
, the Koszul sign rule tensor product
X\otimes Y
carries a
\Sigma_{2}
-action twisted by sign permutation; we need to prove that the
\Sigma_{2}
-action on the graded object
(X\otimes Y)[\bullet]
induced by functoriality, is equivalent to the usual
\Sigma_{2}
-action. In this situation, the
\Sigma_{2}
-action on
(X\otimes Y)[\bullet]
is completely determined by the
\Sigma_{2}
-action on its homotopy groups. The statement then follows from the fact that the induced braiding on the homotopy groups of the tensor product of two objects, satisfies the Koszul sign rule.
∎

Proposition 2.4.9
.

Let
R
be a connective derived commutative ring. There are equivalences

\mathrm{LSym}^{n}_{R}(X[1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{n}_{R}(X)[n],

\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}_{R}(X[-1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{n}_{R}(X)[-n].

for any
n\geq 1
and
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove either statement in the case
R
is a polynomial ring. In this case, since all functors involved are derived from the full subcategory of finitely generated free modules, using base change from
\mathbb{Z}
, it suffices to prove that
\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}[1])\simeq\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}}^{n}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})[n]
for any
d
. This follows from property 2 of Proposition
2.4.8
. The statement for derived divided power algebra follows by duality, as exterior powers commute with taking duals.
∎

Given an
R
-module
X
, let
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(X):=R\oplus\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}(X)
. This is the unital augmented algebra corresponding to the non-unital algebra
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}^{+}(X)
. Also, let
\mathrm{L\Lambda}_{R}(X)
be the free derived exterior algebra on
X
.

Corollary 2.4.10
.

Let
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. There is an equivalence of derived graded
R
-algebras

\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(X[-1])[\bullet]\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}_{R}(X),

where the right hand side is understood as a derived graded algebra with respect to the Koszul sign rule symmetric monoidal structure.

Assume
X^{\geq\star}
is a filtered object in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. The the graded object
\gr^{\bullet}(X)
can be endowed with an additional structure. Namely, for any
n
, there is a cofiber sequence

X^{\geq n+1}\rightarrow X^{\geq n}\rightarrow\gr^{n}(X)

which produces a boundary map
\gr^{n}(X)\rightarrow X^{\geq n+1}[1]
. Composing with the quotient map
X^{\geq n+1}[1]\rightarrow\gr^{n+1}(X)[1]
, we obtain a map
\gr^{n}(X)\rightarrow\gr^{n+1}(X)[1]
or equivalently, a map
\gr^{n}(X)[-1]\rightarrow\gr^{n+1}(X)
. In other words, the associated graded object of a filtered object inherits a graded map
\gr(X)(1)[-1]\rightarrow\gr(X)
encoding successive extensions in the filtration. This structure can be understood in terms of an action of a certain algebra, whose definition we will give below.

Definition 2.4.11
.

Let
R
be a connective derived commutative ring. Following
[
26
]
, we define a square-zero extension algebra

\mathbb{D}_{-}:=R\oplus R[-1](1).

Remark 2.4.12
.

Vanishing of higher exterior powers of a free module of rank
1
together with Proposition
2.4.10
imply that there is an equivalence

\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}\bigl(R[-1](1)\bigr)\simeq\bigl(\mathrm{L\Lambda}_{R}(R)\bigr)[-\bullet]\simeq\bigl(R\oplus R(1)\bigr)[-\bullet]\simeq R\oplus R[-1](1).

The diagonal map
R\rightarrow R\oplus R
induces a map

\mathbb{D}_{-}=\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}\bigl(R[-1](1)\bigr)\rightarrow\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}\bigl(R[-1](1)\bigr)\otimes\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}\bigl(R[-1](1)\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{D}_{-}\otimes\mathbb{D}_{-},

which endows
\mathbb{D}_{-}
with the structure of a
cocommutative Hopf algebra
in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Definition 2.4.13
.

For a connective derived commutative ring
R
, we define the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
of
dg objects in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, or
dg
R
-modules
, as the
\infty
-category of graded modules over the graded algebra
\mathbb{D}_{-}
:

\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{D}_{-}}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}).

Using the cocommutative Hopf algebra structure on
\mathbb{D}_{-}
observed in Remark
2.4.12
, the
\infty
-category gets a symmetric monoidal structure making the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
symmetric monoidal.

Remark 2.4.14
.

The functor
\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
lifts to a symmetric monoidal functor
\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. To explain this, recall that in terms of the Rees equivalence, the functor
\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is equivalent to the base change
-\underset{R[t]}{\otimes}R:\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R[t]}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Consequently, the associated graded object
\gr(X)
of any filtered object
X
carries a natural action of the algebra
\underline{\Hom}_{R[t]}(R,R)
, where the notation
\underline{\Hom}
means the internal graded hom-object. The existence of a natural
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action then follows from the equivalence of graded
R
-algebras

\underline{\Hom}_{R[t]}(R,R)\simeq\underline{\Hom}_{R}(R\underset{R[t]}{\otimes}R,R)\simeq\underline{\Hom}_{R}\bigl(\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(R(-1)[1]),R\bigr)\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}\bigl(R(1)[-1]\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{D}_{-},

We can think of dg modules as representations of a derived algebraic group scheme whose algebra of distributions is
\mathbb{D}_{-}
. In representation theory, there are functors of
homology
and
cohomology
, or speaking in a more higher algebraic language,
homotopy orbits
and
homotopy fixed points
of a representation.

Construction 2.4.15
.

Let
\triv:\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the functor endowing a graded module with a trivial
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action. In other words, it is restricting along the augmentation map
\mathbb{D}_{-}\rightarrow R
. There is an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\triv}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.}
\scriptstyle{(-)_{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}}

For
X\in\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, we call
X_{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}

the
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-homotopy orbits object
of
X
, and
X^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}

homotopy fixed points object
.

Remark 2.4.16
.

Note that as the functor
\triv
is symmetric monoidal, the right adjoint
(-)^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}
is right-lax symmetric monoidal. It is given explicitly by the formula

X^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}:=\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbb{D}_{-}}(R,X),

where the
\underline{\Hom}
is the graded internal hom-object. Similarly as in Remark
2.4.14
, we can compute that there is an equivalence

R^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}=\underline{\Hom}_{\mathbb{D}_{-}}(R,R)=R[t]

of graded algebras, where
t
has degree
-1
. In particular, the graded object
X^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}
carries an operation of degree
-1
, which is the same data as a filtration. We denote

(-)^{\geq\star}:\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}

the resulting functor lifting
(-)^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}
.

We record the following proposition without proof, which can be found in
[
26
, Theorem 3.2.14]
. Let
\widehat{\Fil\mathrm{Mod}}_{R}\subset\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the
\infty
-category of
complete filtered objects
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, i.e. the full subcategory of
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
spanned by the objects
X^{\geq\star}
which satisfy
\underset{n}{\lim}\>X^{\geq n}\simeq 0
.

Theorem 2.4.17
.

Let
R
be a derived commutative ring. The functors
\gr
and
(-)^{h\mathbb{D}_{-}}
form a mutually inverse equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\widehat{\Fil\mathrm{Mod}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gr}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\geq\star}}

Remark 2.4.18
.

If
R
is connective, then the algebra
\mathbb{D}_{-}
is connective in the negative
t
-structure, therefore the negative
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
extends to a
t
-structure on
\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which we call
negative
t
-structure
as well. The heart of this
t
-structure is equivalent to the
abelian category of cochain complexes
of
\pi_{0}(R)
-modules. Note that when
R
is discrete, the algebra
\mathbb{D}_{-}
is discrete in the negative
t
-structure too.

Remark 2.4.19
.

Let us work over
R=\mathbb{Z}
. The structure of a
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action can be understood in stacky terms as follows. Following A.Preygel
[
25
]
, let
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}
be the based loop space of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
defined as a pull-back square in derived affine schemes

\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}:=\{0\}\underset{\mathbb{A}^{1}}{\times}\{0\},

where both maps
\{0\}=\Spec(\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}
are inclusions of the zero section.
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}
becomes a group object in derived schemes with respect to composition of loops. The zero section in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is fixed by the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and induces an action on
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Therefore, we can consider the quotient stack
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
which is a derived group stack over
\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Let
\Rep(\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
be the category of graded
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}
-representations (understood as, say, comodules over the algebra of functions on
\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}
). In these terms, the functor
\textstyle{\gr:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{D}_{-}}}
is given by the pull-back

\textstyle{-\underset{\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}}{\times}\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}:\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Rep(\Omega\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})}

along the inclusion of zero section
i:\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}.

Definition 2.4.20
.

Let
R
be a connective derived ring. Using Remark
2.3.13
and Construction
2.4.2
, there is a notion of derived commutative algebras in
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
,
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
,
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

We let

\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}),\mathrm{DAlg}(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}),\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}),\mathrm{DAlg}(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

to be the respective
\infty
-categories of derived commutative algebras. We will often refer to them as
graded, filtered, non-negatively graded and non-negatively filtered
7
7

7

Note that there is a potential abuse of terminology here as according to standard conventions, a
“graded/filtered derived commutative algebra”
could mean a graded/filtered object in the
\infty
-category of derived commutative algebras. However, in the case of algebras, this seems to be a standard terminology, so there is no risk of confusion. If we did need to speak about filtered/graded objects in derived commutative rings, we would call them by their longer name.

derived commutative algebras
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
respectively, and we use respective notations

\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R},\Fil\mathrm{DAlg}_{R},\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R},\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}.

Below we will give an equivalent, more explicit, description of the
\infty
-categories of derived algebras given in Definition
2.4.20
in the case when
R
is discrete.

Construction 2.4.21
.

Assume
R
is a discrete ring. Let us unwind the natural
t
-structure on
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
obtained from the equivalence
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
. Using the affine map
p:\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m},
an object
F\in\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
is connective iff its push-forward
p_{*}F
is connective as a quasi-coherent sheaf on
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Denote the graded components of
p_{*}F
as
\{F^{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Pulling-back along the map
i:\Spec(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we obtain that
i^{*}p_{*}F
is connective as an
R
-module. But we can identify
i^{*}p_{*}F\simeq F^{0}
, and
F^{0}
is a connective
R
-module. Similarly, by shifting the grading, we obtain that all
F^{i}
’s must be connective
R
-modules. Therefore, the natural
t
-structure on
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})
has the connective part consisting of filtered objects
F^{\geq\star}
which are connective in all degrees. This
t
-structure is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure, and is left and right complete.

Let
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}\subset\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the subcategory spanned by direct sums of objects of the form
R(i)
for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
. This is an additive subcategory of
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
which is closed under the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{0}
. Consequently, using Construction
2.3.14
, we obtain an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
on
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. This action has the property that for any map
f:\Spec A\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the pull-back
f^{*}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\simeq\QCoh(\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m})\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
-linear. In the same way, we construct an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}
on
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Therefore, we obtain two monads on
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
:

1.

The
free filtered derived algebra monad

\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq\star}_{R}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
;

2.

The
free non-unital derived divided power algebra monad

\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+,\geq\star}_{R}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Remark 2.4.22
.

The monad
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq\star}_{R}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
restricts to a filtered-colimit preserving endofunctor
\Sym^{\geq\star}_{R,\heartsuit}:\Ind(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})\rightarrow\Ind(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})
and is uniquely determined by it according to Observation
2.3.15
. The same applies to the monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+,\geq\star}_{R}
.

Let us mention a concrete description of the category
\Ind(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})
.

Proposition 2.4.23
.

The category
\Ind(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})
is spanned by objects
F^{\geq\star}\in\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
where
F^{0}
is a flat
R
-module, and each map
F^{\geq i+1}\hookrightarrow F^{i}
is injective with flat cokernel.

The notion of filtered/graded derived
R
-algebra can be extended to the non-connective case as follows.

Remark 2.4.24
.

Let
R
be a derived ring, and consider
R
as a filtered (graded) derived ring inserted in degree
0
, extended by
0
in positive degrees, and in a constant way in negative degrees. Then we can define

\Fil\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}:=(\Fil\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/},\;\;\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}:=(\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/}.

This recovers the previous definition when
R
is connective, and extends it immediately to the not necessarily connective case.

3
Filtrations and divided power algebras.

3.1
Smith ideals.

Construction 3.1.1
.

Below we will introduce two constructions which feature prominently in the text.

1.

Let
\Delta^{1}
be the
1
-simplex considered as a category with two objects
0,1
and a single non-trivial arrow
0\rightarrow 1
. There is a symmetric monoidal structure
\ast:\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
on
\Delta^{1}
defined as:

0\ast 0=0,0\ast 1=1,1\ast 0=1,1\ast 1=1

(3.1)

Fix a (connective, derived) commutative ring
R
. Then the
\infty
-category
\Fun((\Delta^{1})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
is a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category with respect to the
Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure
. The formula for the tensor product of two objects
X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0}
and
Y^{1}\rightarrow Y^{0}
is as follows:

(X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0})\otimes(Y^{1}\rightarrow Y^{0})\simeq X^{0}\otimes Y^{1}\bigsqcup_{X^{1}\otimes X^{1}}X^{1}\otimes Y^{0}\rightarrow X^{0}\otimes Y^{0}.

We use the notation

\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}:=\Fun((\Delta^{1})^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

considered with Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure satisfying the formula above.

2.

Let
\{0,1\}
be the discrete 2 point category endowed with the symmetric monoidal structure defined on objects by the same formulas as
3.1
. The
\infty
-category
\Fun(\{0,1\}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
gets a Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure with tensor product

(X^{1},X^{0})\otimes(Y^{1},Y^{0})\simeq(X^{0}\otimes Y^{1}\oplus X^{1}\otimes Y^{1}\oplus X^{1}\otimes Y^{0},X^{0}\otimes Y^{0}).

We denote

\mathrm{Mod}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}:=\Fun(\{0,1\}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

considered with Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure.

We will refer to both symmetric monoidal structures defined in the construction above as
“push-out symmetric monoidal structures”
.

Definition 3.1.2
.

Let
R
be a (connective, derived) commutative ring.

1.

Let

\CAlg_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}:=\CAlg(\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R})

be the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras in
\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}
.

2.

Similarly, let

\CAlg_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}:=\CAlg(\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee})

be the
\infty
-category of commutative algebra objects in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
.

In what follows we will clarify the meanings of these two
\infty
-categories. As a warm-up, let us give a description of discrete objects in
\CAlg_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and
\CAlg_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
respectively.

Definition 3.1.3
.

Recall the classical notion of a Smith ideal
8
8

8

This notion was introduced by Jess Smith and was reviewed and used recently in the texts
[
8
]
,
[
22
]
,
[
9
]
and others.
. Let
A
be a discrete commutative ring. A
Smith ideal
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
is an
A
-module
M
endowed with an
A
-linear map
i:I\rightarrow A
such that
i(x)y=xi(y)
. The map
I\otimes_{A}I\rightarrow I
given by sending
(x,y)\longmapsto i(x)y
defines a
non-unital
A
-algebra
structure on
I
, and the quotient
A/I:=\cofib(I\rightarrow A)
has a structure of a simplicial commutative ring, which is in general non-discrete unless
I\rightarrow A
is injective.

Proposition 3.1.4
.

Let
R
be a discrete commutative ring. Let
\CAlg_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and
\CAlg_{R,\heartsuit}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
be the subcategories of
\CAlg_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and
\CAlg_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
respectively, consisting of discrete objects with respect to the canonical
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

1.

The category
\CAlg_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
is equivalent to the category of pairs
(I\rightarrow A)
where
A
is a commutative
R
-algebra, and
i:I\rightarrow A
is a Smith ideal in
A
.

2.

The category
\CAlg_{R,\heartsuit}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
is equivalent to the category of pairs
(A,B)
where
A
is a commutative
R
-algebra, and
B
is a non-unital
A
-algebra.

Proof.

1.

A unital commutative algebra structure on an object
i:A^{1}\rightarrow A^{0}
amounts to giving a unit map
\varepsilon:(0\rightarrow R)\rightarrow(A^{1}\rightarrow A^{0})
, and a multiplication map

\textstyle{A^{1}\otimes A^{0}\underset{A^{1}\otimes A^{1}}{\bigsqcup}A^{0}\otimes A^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m^{1}}
\textstyle{A^{0}\otimes A^{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m^{0}}
\textstyle{A^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{0}}

which satisfy associativity and commutativity conditions. The maps
m^{0}
and
\varepsilon^{0}
give a unital, commutative and associative multiplication on
A^{0}
. The map

m^{1}:A^{1}\otimes A^{0}\bigsqcup_{A^{1}\otimes A^{1}}A^{0}\otimes A^{1}\rightarrow A^{1}

is equivalent to a pair of maps
A^{0}\otimes A^{1}\rightarrow A^{1}
and
A^{1}\otimes A^{0}\rightarrow A^{1}
which agree on
A^{1}\otimes A^{1}
. This defines a left and right actions of
A^{0}
on
A^{1}
satisfying the condition
i(x)y=xi(y)
. Commutativity and associativity constraints say that
A^{1}
is symmetric
A^{0}
-bimodule, and
i:A^{1}\rightarrow A^{0}
is an
A^{0}
-module map. This is the same as a Smith ideal.

2.

The proof of this part goes the same way as in part 1 by unwinding the definition of a commutative algebra object in tensor product defined in Construction
3.1.1
, 2.

∎

Construction 3.1.5
.

Let
R
be a derived commutative ring, and we will consider the
\infty
-category of arrows
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
witht the
pointwise
symmetric monoidal structure. The functor
\cofib:\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
sending
(X\rightarrow Y)\longmapsto(Y\rightarrow\cofib(X\rightarrow Y))
admits an oplax symmetric monoidal structure. To see this, we first note that the functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R},(X\rightarrow Y)\longmapsto Y
is symmetric monoidal. Then it remains to construct an oplax symmetric monoidal structure on the functor
\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R},(X\rightarrow Y)\longmapsto\cofib(X\rightarrow Y).
But this functor is the left adjoint of the symmetric monoidal inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}
which sends
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to
(0\rightarrow X)\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}
, and therefore admits an oplax symmetric monoidal structure.

Proposition 3.1.6
.

The oplax symmetric monoidal structure on the functor
\cofib:\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
is strict.

Proof.

We need to show the following. For two arrows
(X\rightarrow Y)
and
(X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y^{\prime})
the natural map

\textstyle{\cofib(X\otimes Y^{\prime}\underset{X\otimes X^{\prime}}{\bigsqcup}Y\otimes X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\otimes Y^{\prime})\rightarrow Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}

is an equivalence. This is equivalent to showing that the diagram

\textstyle{(Y\otimes Y^{\prime})/(X\otimes X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}}

is a push-out square. This is in turn equivalent to showing that the map induced on horizontal cofibers is an equivalence. The cofiber of the lower horizontal arrow is equivalent to
X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}[1]
. The cofiber of the upper hozitontal arrow can be read of from the diagram

\textstyle{X\otimes X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\otimes f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\Id\otimes f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\otimes Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\otimes\Id}
\textstyle{X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\otimes Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{Y\otimes Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(Y\otimes Y^{\prime})/(X\otimes X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}[1].}

Therefore, the natural map
\cofib((Y\otimes Y^{\prime})/(X\otimes X^{\prime})\rightarrow Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime})\rightarrow\cofib(Y\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime}\rightarrow Y/X\otimes Y^{\prime}/X^{\prime})
is an equivalence, and this finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 3.1.7
.

The functor
\cofib:\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories where the source is endowed with the push-out symmetric monoidal structure, and the target with the pointwise symmetric monoidal structure.

Proof.

The inverse functor sends a map
X\rightarrow Y
to
\fib(X\rightarrow Y)\rightarrow X
.
∎

The following corollary is immediate from Proposition
3.1.7
:

Corollary 3.1.8
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories:

\textstyle{\cofib:\CAlg_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\CAlg_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}.}

Let us now clarify the meaning of the
\infty
-category
\CAlg_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
. As a preparation for the next construction, observe that given an object of
(A^{1},A^{0})\in\CAlg^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R,\heartsuit}
, we can define a Smith ideal by the formula
(A^{1}\rightarrow A^{0}\oplus A^{1})
. On the other hand, any Smith ideal
(I\rightarrow A)
gives rise to a pair
(A,I)\in\CAlg^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R,\heartsuit}
where
I
is considered merely as a non-unital
A
-algebra. Below we will construct an adjunction of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories which gives rise to these constructions on the
\infty
-categories of commutative algebra objects.

Construction 3.1.9
.

The tautological inclusion functor
\{0,1\}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
is strictly symmetric monoidal with respect to the symmetric monoidal structure described in Construction
3.1.1
, and consequently induces an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{aug}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R},}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}

where the left adjoint
\mathrm{aug}
is strictly symmetric monoidal, and the right adjoint
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})
is lax symmetric monoidal.

Unwinding the definition, the functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})
sends an arrow
(Y\rightarrow X)
to the pair
(Y,X)
. The left adjoint
\mathrm{aug}
sends a pair
(X^{1},X^{0})
to the arrow
(X^{1}\rightarrow X^{1}\oplus X^{0})
.

Remark 3.1.10
.

The functor
\mathrm{aug}
preserves all colimits being a left adjoint. It also preserves all limits, as follows from the formula. Moreover, it is conservative: if a map
(X^{1},X^{0})\rightarrow(Y^{1},Y^{0})
induces an equivalence
(X^{1}\rightarrow X^{1}\oplus X^{0})\simeq(Y^{1}\rightarrow Y^{1}\oplus Y^{0})
, then we immediately get that
X^{1}\simeq Y^{1}
is an equivalence, and taking cofibers that
X^{0}\simeq Y^{0}
too. It follows that
\mathrm{aug}
is monadic and comonadic.

Remark 3.1.11
.

It follows from proposition above that the adjunction of Construction
3.1.9
refines to an adjunction

\textstyle{\CAlg^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{aug}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\CAlg^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}.}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}

Based on Remark
3.1.10
, the
\infty
-category
\CAlg^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}
should have a description as an
\infty
-category of objects of
\CAlg^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}
endowed with some additional data. Such a description will be given below.

Construction 3.1.12
.

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}
be the
\infty
-category of functors
\Fun(\{0,1\},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
endowed with the
pointwise
symmetric monoidal structure, i.e. the tensor product of two objects
(X^{0},X^{1})
and
(Y^{0},Y^{1})
is given by the formula

(X^{0},X^{1})\otimes(Y^{0},Y^{1})\simeq(X^{0}\otimes Y^{0},X^{1}\otimes Y^{1}),

and the unit object is the pair
(R,R)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}
.

The symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}
carries a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\varepsilon:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}
defined by the formula
\varepsilon(X^{1},X^{0}):=(X^{0},X^{1})
. We define a stable
\infty
-category of
augmented arrows
as the pull-back

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon\circ(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}.}

(3.2)

Objects of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}}
can be thought as arrows
p:Y\rightarrow X
endowed with a section
e:X\rightarrow Y
such that
p\circ e\simeq\Id_{Y}
. We can endow
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}}
with
pointwise
symmetric monoidal structure, so that the diagram
3.2
is a pull-back diagram in
\CAlg(\mathrm{Cat})_{\mathrm{lax}}
. There exists a symmetric monoidal functor

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}},}
\textstyle{\;\;\;\;\;(X^{1},X^{0})\longmapsto(X^{0}\oplus X^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces X^{0})}
\scriptstyle{e}

(3.3)

where the arrow
p:(X^{1}\oplus X^{0}\rightarrow X^{0})
is the natural projection, endowed with the
0
-section
e:X^{0}\hookrightarrow X^{1}\oplus X^{0}
.

Proposition 3.1.13
.

The functor
3.3
induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}}.}

Proof.

The inverse functor sends a split arrow
Z\rightarrow X
to the pair
(Y,X)
, where
Y:=\fib(Z\rightarrow X)
.
∎

Corollary 3.1.14
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\CAlg_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.875pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\CAlg^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{aug}}_{R}:=\CAlg(\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{aug}}_{R})

Remark 3.1.15
.

Given the description of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{aug}}
as a pull-back diagram
3.2
in
\CAlg(\mathrm{Cat})_{\mathrm{lax}}
, Corollary
3.1.14
together with Proposition
2.1.2
imply that the
\infty
-category
\CAlg^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}:=\CAlg(\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee})
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of arrows
p:B\rightarrow A
in
\CAlg_{R}
endowed with a section
i:A\rightarrow B
, or equivalently, pairs
(A,A^{+})
where
A\in\CAlg_{R}
and
A^{+}\in\CAlg^{\mathrm{nu}}_{A}
.

We will now move to the study of Smith ideals in the derived world.

Construction 3.1.16
.

We define a derived algebra object
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
as an arrow
A\rightarrow B
in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
. The resulting
\infty
-category
\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{DAlg}_{R})
is monadic over
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
, and when
R
is discrete, we can describe the monad as the right-left extension of the monad of free commutative algebra defined on the subcategory
(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
using the standard (pointwise)
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. We denote this monad as
(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. It is uniquely determined by its restriction to the full subcategory
\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
consisting of pairs
(f:X\rightarrow Z)
with both
X
and
Z
being flat
R
-modules.

Below we will give another construction of the
\infty
-category of derived algebras in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
, which is based on a different
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
and another choice of a subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
.

Construction 3.1.17
.

Let
R
be a discrete ring. The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
has a
geometric
t
-structure
, whose connective part
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}}
consists of arrows
(f:X\rightarrow Z)
with both
X
and
Z
being connective, and the map
f
being surjective on
\pi_{0}
. Equivalently, an arrow
(f:X\rightarrow Z)
is in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}}
if
X
and
\fib(f)
are connective. Consequently, an arrow
(f:X\rightarrow Z)
is coconnective in geometric
t
-structure if
X
and
\fib(f)
are coconnective. Below we will list the main observations regarding this
t
-structure.

•

The geometric
t
-structure is compatible with the pointwise symmetric monoidal structure, and is left and right complete.

•

The geometric
t
-structure is obtained by transferring the standard (pointwise)
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
by means of the self-equivalence
\cofib:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
.

•

The heart
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}}
consists of arrows
(f:X\twoheadrightarrow Z)
for which
X
and
\fib(f)
are discrete
R
-modules. Note that
Z
itself might not be discrete, as long as
f:X\rightarrow Z
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
.

•

There is an additive symmetric monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{surj}}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}}
consisting of surjective maps
(f:X\twoheadrightarrow Z)
where both
X
and
Z
are discrete.

•

Let
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}}\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}}
be the subcategory spanned by direct sums of objects
R\rightarrow 0
and
\Id:R\rightarrow R
. Then
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}}
is an additive subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}}
, and it is closed under the action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{0}
and
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen},\mathrm{pd}}_{0}
. Moreover, the objects
R\rightarrow 0
and
\Id:R\rightarrow R
are compact and projective generators of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}
. Consequently, using Construction
2.3.14
, we obtain an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
on
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. In particular, we get an
\mathrm{LSym}
monad
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}:\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. We denote the
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}
-algebras as
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
and refer to its objects
derived (
R
-linear) arrows
. A justification for this terminology will be given below when we show that this is indeed the same
\infty
-category as the one defined in Construction
3.1.16
.

•

The additive subcategory
\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})\subset\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
is spanned by objects of the form
(f:X\twoheadrightarrow Z)
where
X
and
Z
are flat
R
-modules. The monad
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}
is uniquely determined by its restriction to
\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})
.

•

There exists a tautological forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{DAlg}_{R})
. To construct it, recall that in Construction
3.1.16
we let
(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}}
to be the category of all arrows of finitely generated free
R
-modules; there is an inclusion
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}}\subset(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}}
, which induces a colimit-preserving fully faithful functor
\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})\hookrightarrow\Ind((\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}})
making commutative the following diagram:

\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}}
\textstyle{\Ind((\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}}
\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\Delta^{1}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Ind((\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})^{\Delta^{1}}).}

Consequently, by right-left extension we obtain a map of monads
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\rightarrow(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}
, and a functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\rightarrow\Fun(\Delta^{1},\mathrm{DAlg}_{R})
.

Proposition 3.1.18
.

The functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\rightarrow(\mathrm{DAlg}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

The forgetful functor is a functor of monadic
\infty
-categories over
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. Both monads preserve sifted colimits and are filtered by subfunctors which preserve finite totalizations, the monad
(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}
is determined by its values on the objects
0\rightarrow R
and
R\rightarrow 0
, and we have an equivalence
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}(R\rightarrow 0)\simeq(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}(R\rightarrow 0)
as
R\rightarrow 0
