belongs to the connective part of the geometric
t
-structure. Therefore, it only remains to show that the natural map

\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}(0\rightarrow R)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}(0\rightarrow R)

(3.4)

is an equivalence.

The object
(0\rightarrow R)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
is connective in the standard
t
-structure, and the free
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}
-algebra on it is the unit map
(R\rightarrow R[x]).
On the other hand,
(0\rightarrow R)
is not connective in the geometric
t
-structure. To compute
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}(0\rightarrow R)
, we can present
(0\rightarrow R)
as a totalization

(0\rightarrow R)\simeq(0\rightarrow R[1])[-1]\simeq\underset{n\in\Delta}{\Tot}(0\rightarrow R[1]^{\oplus^{n}}),

(3.5)

and use the colimit formula of Construction
2.2.12

\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}:=\underset{i}{\colim}\;\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1},\leq i}_{R},

(3.6)

where each filtered layer
\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1},\leq i}_{R}
commutes with finite totalizations being an
i
-excisive functor.

Since
(0\rightarrow R[1]^{\oplus^{n}})
is connective in geometric
t
-structure, we have by definition of the
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
functor:

\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}(0\rightarrow R[1]^{\oplus^{n}})\simeq(R\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(R[1]^{\oplus^{n}}).

Consequently, combining formulas
3.5
and
3.6
, we obtain

\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}(0\rightarrow R)\simeq\underset{i}{\colim}\;\underset{n\in\Delta}{\Tot}\Bigl(R\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}^{\leq i}_{R}(R^{\oplus^{n}}[1])\Bigr).

As the map
3.4
is compatible with filtrations, and the same totalization formula works for the standard
(\mathrm{LSym}_{R})^{\Delta^{1},\leq n}
-functors, we conclude that it is an equivalence.
∎

Example 3.1.19
.

Let
R=\mathbb{Z}
. We compute in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}
:

\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Id:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z})\simeq(\Id:\mathbb{Z}[x]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[x]);

\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow 0)\simeq(\mathbb{Z}[x]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z},x\longmapsto 0).

The corresponding Smith ideals in the polynomial ring on one generator are
(0)\subset\mathbb{Z}[x]
and
(x)\subset\mathbb{Z}[x]
respectively.

Remark 3.1.20
.

When
R
is a discrete commutative ring, the initial object in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
is the arrow
(\Id:R\rightarrow R)
. Therefore, when
R
is a not necessarily discrete derived algebra, we can define the
\infty
-category of geometric
R
-linear arrows as the under-category

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}:=(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}})_{R/},

where
R
is considered as a tautological arrow
(\Id:R\rightarrow R)
in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}
.

Definition 3.1.21
.

Let

be a connective derived ring, and

be the

There is an adjunction

Remark 3.1.23
.

Given a map

For a connective derived ring

Construction 3.1.24
.

Let
\Delta^{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
be the inclusion of
0
and
1
. Notice that it is a lax symmetric monoidal functor with respect to the symmetric monoidal structure on

described in Construction

. It induces a limit preserving lax symmetric monoidal functor on presheaf categories

which sends an object

to the arrow
F^{\geq 1}\rightarrow F^{\geq 0}
. In fact, the lax symmetric monoidal structure is strict. Indeed, composing the functor

, we get the functor sending a filtered object

to the arrow

, which is manifestly symmetric monoidal. Therefore, so is the functor
\ev^{[0,1]}
.

•

Assume

is continuous and sends

\textstyle{\Ind(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\geq\star}}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R,0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}}
\textstyle{\Ind(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R,0}).}

\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}.

If

and zero in all other degrees, and notice that

.

Remark 3.1.25
.

Using Definition
3.1.21
, let us describe limits and sifted colimits in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
. In the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
limits and colimits are taken pointwise. Let
S\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}},i\longmapsto(A_{i},I_{i})
be a diagram in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
. Applying the functor
\cofib:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
, we get a diagram
\{A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}/I_{i}\}
in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
. Let
(A\rightarrow A/I):=\lim_{i}(A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}/I_{i})
. Applying the functor
\fib
, we get that the colimit of the diagram
\{(A_{i},I_{i})\}
is given by
(I,A)
, where
A=\lim_{i}A_{i}
and

I=\fib(\lim_{i}A\rightarrow\lim_{i}A_{i}/I_{i}))\simeq\lim_{i}\fib(A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}/I_{i})\simeq\lim_{i}I_{i},

where each term
I_{i}
is endowed with the
A
-module structure obtained by restricting via the natural map
A\rightarrow A_{i}
.

Similarly, let
(A^{\prime},I^{\prime})=\colim_{i}(A_{i},I_{i})
. Then
A^{\prime}=\colim_{i}A_{i}
, and
I^{\prime}
is computed as

I^{\prime}=\fib(\colim_{i}A_{i}\rightarrow\colim_{i}A_{i}/I_{i}).

Assume
S
is a sifted diagram. Then as the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
commutes with sifted colimits, and in the stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
finite limits commute with sifted colimits, we obtain that

I^{\prime}=\colim_{i}(I_{i}\otimes_{A_{i}}A^{\prime})

as an
A^{\prime}
-module.

Remark 3.1.26
.

Consider the category
\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{inj}}
of pairs
(I\rightarrow A)
where
A
is a discrete commutative ring, and
I\hookrightarrow A
is a usual ideal. One can show that
\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{inj}}
has all colimits, but the forgetful functor
\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{inj}}\rightarrow\CAlg_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit},(I\rightarrow A)\longmapsto A
does not commute with colimits. An explicit example is worked out in
[
22
, Example 3.33]
. The computation of Zhouhang Mao is motivated by
[
1
, Remark 23.3.5]
, where the same example is brought to demonstrate the discrepancy between coproducts in the category of divided power algebras and tensor products of underlying algebras. In the setting of derived Smith ideals the same problem does not arise, even in the divided power case.

We will finish this subsection with the following construction which will be useful in the sequel.

Construction 3.1.27
.

Let
\mathrm{ins}^{0}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
be the functor sending
A
the trivial Smith ideal
0\rightarrow A
of
A
. There is an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ins}^{0}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}},}
\scriptstyle{\gr^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}

where
\gr^{0}(I\rightarrow A)=A/I
and
\ev^{0}(I\rightarrow A)=A
.

3.2
Divided power algebras.

Based on Definition
3.1.21
, given a map
A\rightarrow B
, we can construct a unique derived Smith ideal
I\rightarrow A
in
A
, and formulate various properties or additional structures on the map
A\rightarrow B
in terms of the Smith ideal
I
. In this text we will define
divided power structure
on a map of derived commutative rings as the data of a non-unutal derived divided power structure on the underlying non-unital derived commutative algebra of
I
. Below we shall explain our reasons for introducing divided power algebras.

There are two ways to think about De Rham cohomology theory, a more well-known one is in terms of differential graded commutative algebras, and another one is in terms of divided power algebras. Assume
A
is a discrete commutative algebra. Then there is a classical universal property of the De Rham complex of
A
: for any map
A\rightarrow B^{0}
into the
0
-th graded piece of a differential graded commutative algebra
B^{\bullet}
, there is a unique map of differential graded commutative algebras
\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}\rightarrow B^{\bullet}
. Moreover, we have an equivalence of graded algebras
\Omega^{\bullet}_{A}\simeq\Lambda^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega^{1}_{A})
with the differential extended from the universal derivation
d:A\rightarrow\Omega_{A}^{1}
via the graded Leibnitz rule. A.Raksit
[
26
]
generalized this universal property to the derived setting. In this text we will develop another approach in terms of derived
divided power
algebras, rather than differential graded commutative algebras. It can be briefly summarized as follows. One defines an
\infty
-category
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
of
non-negatively filtered derived divided power algebras
, and considers the functor
\gr^{0}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
. The derived De Rham complex
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{-/R}
is defined as the left adjoint of this functor. The main reason why this recovers the classical De Rham complex in the case of a smooth map
R\rightarrow A
, is essentially due to the décalage isomorphism

\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}(M[-1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{n}(M)[-n]

demonstrated in Proposition
2.4.10
. Namely, the associated graded object of
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}
is given by

\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\bullet}_{A/R}\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\bullet}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{\bullet}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A})[-n],

where
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
is the cotangent complex of the map
R\rightarrow A
, and in the case when
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
is a projective
A
-module, the divided power structure on
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}
is equivalent to the data of a differential graded commutative algebra. The main reason why we prefer to use think about De Rham cohomology in terms of derived divided power algebras, is because various constructions are more natural and easier to define in this setting. This particularly applies to stacky approaches to derived De Rham cohomology, relation with crystalline and prismatic cohomology. We will show that the relative derived De Rham cohomology
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/B}
of a map
A\rightarrow B
can be understood as the
derived pd filtration
of the
derived pd envelope
of
A
with respect to the Smith ideal
I=\fib(A\rightarrow B)
. In the case when the map
A\rightarrow B
is a surjective map of discrete commutative algebras whose ideal
I
is generated by a regular sequence, this recovers the classical divided power envelope. On the other hand, when the map
A\rightarrow B
is a smooth map of discrete commutative rings, the negative
t
-structure together with décalage isomorphism enables us to think about the relative De Rham complex
\Omega_{A/B}
in terms of the exterior algebra on the module of relative Kahler differentials
\Omega^{1}_{B/A}
.

We will finish this introduction with a historical remark. Divided power algebras featured prominently in algebraic topology, i.e. in the context of homology of loop spaces, and were brought to world of algebraic geometry by A.Grothendieck
[
13
]
in the context of crystalline cohomology. The basic idea of divided power algebras is that outside of characteristic
0
, we can not form
\frac{x^{n}}{n!}
of an element in an arbitrary algebra
A
, and a divided power structure on
A
is an additional structure which axiomatizes the existence of such operations on some ideal. Initially, the definition of a divided power algebra was given in terms of the data of operations
\gamma_{n}(x)
for all
n
satisfying a number of conditions reflecting the idea that
\gamma_{n}(x)
should behave like
\frac{x^{n}}{n!}
. B.Fresse in the text
[
10
]
explained an operadic approach to divided power algebras. The manuscript of Brantner, Campos and Nuiten
[
6
]
developed the notion of derived pd operads in a very general setting, which we follow in this text.

3.2.1
Non-unital divided power algebras.

Recall the following classical definition.

Definition 3.2.1
.

A
(discrete) non-unital divided power ring
is a non-unital commutative ring
A
endowed with operations
\gamma_{n}:A\rightarrow A
satisfying the following relations:

1.

\gamma_{0}(x)=1,\gamma_{1}(x)=x
for any
x\in A
;

2.

\gamma_{n}(x+y)=\sum_{i+j=n}\gamma_{i}(x)\gamma_{j}(y)
for any
x,y\in A
;

3.

\gamma(ax)=a^{n}\gamma(x)
for any
a,x\in A
;

4.

\gamma_{m}(x)\gamma_{n}(x)=\frac{(m+n)!}{m!n!}\gamma_{m+n}(x)
;

5.

\gamma_{n}(\gamma_{m}(x))=\frac{(mn)!}{(m!)^{n}n!}\gamma_{mn}(x)
.

We let
\CAlg^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
to be the category of discrete
non-unital
9
9

9

There is no notion of a
“unital”
divided power algebra, because the unit never supports divided powers unless we are in characteristic
0
divided power rings.

Remark 3.2.2
.

It was observed by B.Fresse in
[
10
]
that this category is monadic over
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
with the monad given by the formula

\Gamma^{+}(M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}\Gamma^{n}(M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}(M^{\otimes^{n}})^{\Sigma_{n}}.

There is a
norm map

\Sym(M)\rightarrow\Gamma^{+}(M)
given in every degree by the map
\Sym^{n}(M)\rightarrow\Gamma^{n}(M)
induced by the
\Sigma_{n}
-norm map

\Nm_{\Sigma_{n}}:M^{\otimes^{n}}\rightarrow M^{\otimes^{n}},x_{1}\otimes...\otimes x_{n}\longmapsto\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma_{n}}x_{\sigma(1)}\otimes...\otimes x_{\sigma(n)}

One can think of the structure of a non-unital divided power algerba
A
as a non-unital commutative algebra endowed with a factorization of the multiplication maps
m_{n}:A^{\otimes^{n}}\rightarrow A
through the norm as follows

\textstyle{A^{\otimes^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Nm_{\Sigma_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{m_{n}}
\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{A^{\otimes^{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{n}}

for any
n
, and these factorizations satisfy some additional associativity conditions.

We call
\Gamma^{+}(M)
the
free non-unital divided power algebra
on
M
. There is a subcategory
\CAlg^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}-\mathrm{poly}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\CAlg^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\heartsuit}
of compact projective generators consisting of free non-unital divided power algebras on finitely many variables. One can then define an
\infty
-category

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}:=\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\CAlg^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}-\mathrm{poly}}_{\mathbb{Z}})

which is monadic over
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
, and one can show that it is also comonadic over
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
(see
3.2.6
for precise argument).

Example 3.2.3
.

The free non-untal divided power algebra on one generator
\Gamma^{+}(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+}
can be described as the smallest non-unital subalgebra of
\mathbb{Q}[x]
containing all elements of the form
\frac{x^{n}}{n!}.\in\mathbb{Q}[x]
.

Remark 3.2.4
.

Let
(-)^{\sharp}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
be the right adjoint of the forgetful functor. For any connective
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
, there is an equivalence of spaces

A^{\sharp}\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}}(\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+},A).

For a discrete
A
, the latter can be understood as the set of
divided power sequences
in
A
, i.e. the subset of
\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}A
consisting of collections of elements
(x_{1},x_{2},...)
satisfying

x_{n}x_{m}=\frac{(m+n)!}{m!\>n!}x_{n+m}.

Definition 3.2.5
.

Let
R
be a connective derived commutative ring. We define the
\infty
-category of
(non-unital) derived divided power algebras

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{R}:=\Alg_{\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

as the
\infty
-category of algebras over the non-unital derived divided power algebra monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}
provided by Construction
2.3.11
.

Proposition 3.2.6
.

Let
R
be a connective derived commutative ring, and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the
\infty
-category of non-unital derived commutative
R
-algebras. The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R}
commutes with limits and colimits.

Proof.

We argue as in the proof in
[
26
, Proposition 4.2.27]
. Commutation with limits follows from the fact that both sides are
\infty
-categories of algebras over a monad, and the forgetful functor is restriction along a monad map. Commutation with sifted colimits follows from commutation of the monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
with sifted colimits. For commutation with finite coproducts, one reduces to the statement that for two objects
X,Y\in\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{free},\mathrm{fg}}_{R}
, the natural map
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}(X)\otimes\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}(Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}(X\oplus Y)
is an equivalence, which follows from the case
R=\mathbb{Z}
by base change.
∎

Definition 3.2.7
.

We let
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
and
\mathrm{coEnv}^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
be the left adjoint and the right adjoint of the forgetful functor, respectively. We call them
divided power (pd) envelope
and
divided power (pd) coenvelope
.

Construction 3.2.8
.

Let
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}\subset\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
be the full subcategory of objects whose underlying
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-object is connective. As the free derived divided power algebra monad
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
preserves connective objects, it follows that the functor
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{R}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}
, left adjoint of the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}
, is well-defined, and commutes with the inclusion
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
. Since the inclusion commutes with all colimits, it follows that there exists a right adjoint
connective cover
functor
\tau_{\geq 0}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}
which fits into a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\geq 0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R,\geq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{\geq 0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R},}

where the vertical functors are forgetful functors.

In what follows we will also need to work with divided power algebras over not necessarily connective derived rings. To explain how to do it, consider how this is done in the context of ordinary (without divided powers) derived rings. First, we have the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
of all derived rings. Then given a not necessarily connective
R\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we can consider the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}:=(\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/}
of under objects. For connective
R
, this is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of algebras over the monad
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}
. Over a not necessarily connective
R
, this
\infty
-category is monadic over
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, and we can consider this to be the definition of the monad
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}
for not necessarily connective
R
. We can work similarly in the setting of non-unital derived divided power algebras.

Definition 3.2.9
.

Recall that the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of non-unital derived rings is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of augmented derived rings. Composing the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
with the equivalence
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we obtain a forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}
sending
A^{+}\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}
to the pair
(\mathbb{Z}\oplus A^{+},A^{+})
. Let
R
be a derived ring, not necessarily connective. Consider it as an object
(R,0)
of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}
with
0
augmentation ideal. Let
(\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/}
the
\infty
-category of derived pairs receiving a map from
(R,0)
. Then the
\infty
-category of
derived divided power non-unital
R
-algebras
can be defined as the
\infty
-category of derived non-unital divided power
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras whose underlying augmented derived ring
(\mathbb{Z}\oplus A^{+},A^{+})
receives a map from
(R,0)
:

\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}:=\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}\underset{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}}}{\times}(\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/}.

There is a forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
which is monadic. We denote
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}^{+}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
the free algebra monad.

Remark 3.2.10
.

If
R
is connective, then Definition
3.2.9
is equivalent to Definition
3.2.5
. To see this, we can assume that
R
is discrete, and then it is sufficient to describe the category of discrete divided power
R
-algebras. Then any object of the category defined as in Definition
3.2.9
gives rise to a divided power
\mathbb{Z}
-algerba
A^{+}
together with an action of
R
on
A^{+}
turning
A^{+}
commuting with divided power operations in the usual sense. The slogan is that a divided power
R
-algebra is the same as a divided power ring together with a
R
-linear structure on the underlying non-unital ring. This will be often used throughout the text.

3.2.2
Divided power Smith ideals.

A global version of the notion of a non-unital divided power algebra is the notion of a divided power pair, or a divided power Smith ideal. To define this notion, recall that for any Smith ideal
I
in
A
, the
A
-module
I=\fib(A\rightarrow A/I)
gets a natural structure of a non-unital derived commutative algebra in
A
-modules. A homotopy coherent version of this construction is provided by the functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
induced by the lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
from Construction
3.1.9
. As we observed in Remark
3.1.23
, the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of augmented derived
R
-algebras, i.e. pairs
(A,A^{+})
where
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
and
A^{+}\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. In Definition
3.2.9
we explained how to define non-unital derived divided power algebras over a not necessarily connective base ring. This enables us to give the following definition.

Definition 3.2.11
.

We define an
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
as the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,A^{+})
where
A^{+}
has the structure of a non-unital divided power
A
-algebra in the sense of Definition
3.2.9
. There is a non-conservative forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
(projection on the first component), and a conservative forgetful functor
\mathrm{forget}_{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
, forgetting the divided power structure on the second component. The left adjoint of the latter
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}
sends a pair
(A,A^{+})
to
(A,\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(A^{+}))
, where
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(A^{+}
is the non-unital pd envelope of the non-unital
A
-algebra
A^{+}
. The forgetful functor
\mathrm{forget}_{\mathrm{pd}}
is monadic, and so is the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
. We denote
(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}):\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
the corresponding monad, which satisfies the formula

(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+})(X,Y)\simeq(\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X),\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)}(Y\otimes\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X))).

If
R
is discrete, this monad is right-left extended from its restriction to the full subcategory
\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R,0})
, where
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
is the additive subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
consisting of objects of the form
(X,Y)
, where both
X
and
Y
are finitely generated free
R
-modules.

Definition 3.2.12
.

Let
(I\rightarrow A)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
. A
divided power (pd) structure
on
I
is a lift of the non-unital derived commutative
A
-algebra
I
to a non-unital derived divided power
A
-algebra. In this situation, we call
(I\rightarrow A)
a
divided power (pd) pair
, and
I
a
divided power Smith ideal
. They form an
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
defined as the pull-back

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}_{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee},}

where both functors involved in the diagram are forgetful functors.

We also say that an arrow
(A\rightarrow B)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
has the structure of a
divided power map
if the fiber
\fib(A\rightarrow B)
is given the structure of a non-uinital divided power
A
-algebra. We let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}
be the
\infty
-category of divided power maps. By construction, there is an equivalence

\textstyle{\cofib:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}.}

Proposition 3.2.13
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
is presentable, and the forgetful fuctor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
preserves limits and sifted colimits.

Proof.

The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
is presentable being a pull-back of presentable
\infty
-categories along accessible functors. Let
\mathcal{D}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}},(i\in\mathcal{D})\longmapsto(A_{i},I_{i})
be a diagram. Let
(I\rightarrow A):=\underset{i\in\mathcal{D}}{\lim}(A_{i},I_{i})
be the limit taken in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
. The Smith ideal
I=\lim I_{i}
has the structure of a non-unital divided power
A
-algebra as the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\mathrm{nu}}
creates limits. Therefore,
(I\rightarrow A)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
. Let us show that the object
(I\rightarrow A)
satisfies the universal property. Assume
(B,J)\rightarrow(A_{i},I_{i})
is a collection of maps into the diagram
\mathcal{D}
. We have a pull-back diagram

\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}}((B,J),(I\rightarrow A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}},\inf}((B,J),(I\rightarrow A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}}((B,J),(I\rightarrow A))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}}((B,J),(I\rightarrow A)),}

each term of which is equivalent to the limit
\underset{\mathcal{D}}{\lim}\Map((B,J),(A_{i},I_{i}))
computed in the corresponding
\infty
-category. Therefore, we have

\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}}((B,J),(I\rightarrow A))\simeq\underset{\mathcal{D}}{\lim}\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}}((B,J),(A_{i},I_{i}))

as well, and
(I\rightarrow A)
is the limit of the diagram in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
, as desired. The same argument shows that the forgetful functor also commutes with sifted colimits.

∎

Definition 3.2.14
.

We define the functor of
derived divided power (pd) envelope

\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
as the left adjoint of the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
. By commutation with sifted colimits and conservativity, it follows that the forgetful functors
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
are monadic.

Proposition 3.2.15
.

The derived divided power envelope functor
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
sends the full subcategory
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\subset\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
to
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\subset\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}

Proof.

By passing to right adjoints, the statement is equivalent to the statement that for a divided power pair
(I\rightarrow A)
, the connective cover
(\tau_{\geq 0}A,\tau_{\geq 0}I)
is again a divided power pair. This comes down to the connective cover
\tau_{\geq 0}I
being defined as a non-unital divided power
\tau_{\geq 0}A
-algebra. This is the content of Construction
3.2.8
.
∎

We gave a rather abstract definition of the
\infty
-category of divided power Smith ideals, without using any explicit compact projective generators. As a result, it is apriori unclear what free algebras in this theory look like. The next Proposition affirms that nothing
“surprising”
happens.

Proposition 3.2.16
.

There are equivalences of divided power Smith ideals:

•

\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}(\Id:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle,

•

\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}(0\rightarrow\mathbb{Z})\simeq(0)\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[x],

where
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle
is as defined in Example
3.2.3
.

Proof.

We will prove the first statement only, as the proof of the second is similar. The proof goes by observing that the objects
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}(\Id:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z})
lifts to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}
of non-unital divided power algebras by means of the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}.\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+}_{\mathbb{Z}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{aug}}

where the left vertical functor sends
X\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
to
(\Id:X\rightarrow X)\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
, and the right vertical functor sends a non-unital derived divided power algerba
A^{+}
to the pd Smith ideal
A^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\oplus A^{+}
. Commutativity of this diagram follows by passing to right adjoints, as the right adjoints of horizontal functors are forgetful functors, the right adjoint of the left vertical is the functor
\ev^{1}
evaluating at the source of the arrow, and the right adjoint of the right vertical is the functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})
which takes a Smith ideal
(I\rightarrow A)
to the underlying non-unital derived pd algebra of
I
. As we know that the free non-unutal divided power algebra on one generator is
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+}
, and the corresponding pd Smith ideal is
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle
, the statement follows.
∎

Proposition 3.2.17
.

Let
X\rightarrow Z
be an object of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}}
, and
Y=\fib(X\rightarrow Z)
. Let
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)
be the free object in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
on
X\rightarrow Z
. There is an equivalence

\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\Bigl(\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)\Bigr)\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Y)\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y)}{\otimes}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)

as objects of
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
.

Proof.

Note that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Y)\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y)}{\otimes}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z),}

where the map
f
is induced by the Norm map
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y)
. To produce a natural map from the derived divided power envelope, we need to show that the fiber of the map

\varepsilon:\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Y)\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y)}{\otimes}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)

has the structure of a divided power Smith ideal. This can be done by observing that formation of the map
\varepsilon
is functorial in the fiber sequence
Y\rightarrow X\rightarrow Z
and commutes with sifted colimits, and we ran resolve the fiber sequence
Y\rightarrow X\rightarrow Z
as a sifted colimit of split fiber sequences of the form
Y_{i}\rightarrow Y_{i}\oplus Z_{i}\rightarrow Z_{i}
. It then suffices to show that the map

\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Z_{i})\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z_{i})}{\otimes}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i}\oplus Z_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})

is naturally a divided power map. But we can identify it with the map
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Z_{i})\otimes\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})
which is the tensor product of maps
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Z_{i})\rightarrow R
and
\Id:\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})
, so the fiber of the map
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Z_{i})\otimes\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})
is
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}^{\geq 1}(Z_{i})\otimes\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y_{i})
which is a non-unital divided power algebra. Therefore, the map
\varepsilon
is a divided power map, and therefore there exists a map

\textstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\Bigl(\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)\Bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{R}(Y)\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Y)}{\otimes}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(X)\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(Z)}

by the universal property of derived divided power envelope. To check that this is an equivalence, since
X
and
Z
are connective, we can resolve the map
X\rightarrow Z
by finitely generated free
R
-modules, and by base change deduce the statement from Remark
3.2.16
.
∎

Proposition 3.2.18
.

The forgetful functor
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
commutes with all colimits.

Proof.

To show that it commutes with finite coproducts. Here again, we argue as in
[
26
, Proposition 4.2.27]
. Using formulas of Proposition
3.2.17
, we see that the maps

\textstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\bigl(\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}(0:0\rightarrow X)\bigr)\otimes\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\bigl(\mathrm{LSym}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}(\Id:Z\rightarrow Z)\bigr)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\bigl((0:0\rightarrow X)\oplus(\Id:Z\rightarrow Z)\bigr)}

are equivalences for all
X,Z\in\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{fg},\mathrm{free}}
. From this, we conclude that
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
transforms directs sums of compact projective generators of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
to tensor products, and commutation of the forgetful functor with coproducts follows.
∎

Definition 3.2.19
.

It follows from Proposition
3.2.18
that there is an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}},}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{coEnv}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

where we call the left adjoint the
derived divided power (pd) envelope
, and the right adjoint
derived divided power (pd) coenvelope
.

Remark 3.2.20
.

The adjunction of Construction
3.1.27
lifts to an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ins}^{0}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}.}
\scriptstyle{\gr^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}

In particular, there is a commutative square

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ins}^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{ins}^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}.}

Passing to left and right adjoints, we obtain commutative squares

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\gr^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}

\scriptstyle{\gr^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Id}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{coEnv}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

Remark 3.2.21
.

Let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\subset\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
be the inclusion of the full subcategory of all (divided power) Smith ideals consisting of objects
(I\rightarrow A)
with both
A
and
I
being connective. Then there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}},}

where the vertical functors are inclusions. In other words, divided power envelopes preserve connective Smith ideals. Commutativity of the diagram follows by passing to right adjoints as the right adjoint of inclusion is connective cover which manifestly commutes with forgetful functors.

We let
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
be the
\infty
-category of divided power maps
A\rightarrow B
from
A
, and
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
be the
\infty
-category of divided power maps
C\rightarrow A
to
A
. Concretely, they are defined as fiber products:

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R//A,\geq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{1}}
\textstyle{\{A\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}.}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/,\geq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}
\textstyle{\{A\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R,\geq 0}}

Proposition 3.2.22
.

There exist adjunctions

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A/}^{\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{A/}\;\;,}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{coEnv}_{A/}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{forget}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{/A}.}
\scriptstyle{\Env_{/A}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

The functor
\mathrm{coEnv}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A/}
preserves connective objects. If
B\rightarrow A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{A/,\geq 0}
is surjective on
\pi_{0}
, then the divided power envelope
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}_{/A}(B)
is connective again.

Proof.

Both statement follow from Remark
3.2.20
.
∎

Proposition 3.2.23
.

For a derived pair
I\rightarrow A
, the divided power coenvelope
\mathrm{coEnv}^{\mathrm{pd}}(I\rightarrow A)
is equivalent to the pair
I^{\sharp}\rightarrow A
, where
I^{\sharp}
is as in Remark
3.2.4
.

Definition 3.2.24
.

In the particular case
I=A
considered as the tautological Smith ideal
\Id:A\rightarrow A
, the divided power ideal
I^{\sharp}
will be called
divided power (pd) radical of
R
.

We will finish this subsection with the following easy observation relating our approach to derived divided power Smith ideals with the
\infty
-category of animated pd pairs of Z.Mao
[
22
]
.

Proposition 3.2.25
.

Let
\mathrm{C}^{0}
be the full subcategory of all divided power Smith ideals consisting of finite tensor products of the objects
(0)\subset\mathbb{Z}[x]
and
\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}\langle x\rangle
(see Proposition
3.2.16
). Then there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{C}^{0}).

Proof.

Indeed, by monadicity of
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
over
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and preservation of sifted colimits by the monad, it follows that compact projective generators of
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
are the free objects on finite coproducts of
0\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
and
\Id:\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}
(the latter being compact projective generators of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
). Since coproducts in
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}
are the same as tensor products, the statement follows from the computation of Proposition
3.2.16
.
∎

3.2.3
Filtered divided power algebras.

In this subsection we will define filtered divided power algebras. A typical derived filtered divided power algebra
F^{\geq\star}A
looks like a filtered derived algebra together with a divided power structure on the
“filtered Smith ideal”

F^{\geq 1}A:=\fib(F^{\geq\star}A\rightarrow\gr^{0}A)
. To make this precise, a necessary step is to work out the definition of filtered derived power non-unital algebras over a general derived ring. As an intermediate step for this, we will introduce an auxilary construction in the world of ordinary filtered derived rings.

Construction 3.2.26
.

Let
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
be the
\infty
-category of filtered derived rings. Given any object
F^{\geq\star}A
, the derived ring
F^{\geq 0}A
acts on all higher stages of the filtration
F^{\geq i}A
. Assume that the map
F^{\geq 0}A\rightarrow\gr^{0}A
admits a derived algebra splitting
\gr^{0}A\rightarrow F^{\geq 0}A
. Then
F^{\geq 0}A
becomes an augmented derived ring
F^{\geq 0}A\simeq\gr^{0}A\oplus F^{\geq 1}A
endowed with a filtered augmentation ideal
F^{\geq 1}A
, on which
\gr^{0}A
acts via the section
\gr^{0}A\rightarrow F^{\geq 0}A
. Let
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
be the
\infty
-category of filtered derived rings
F^{\geq 0}A
endowed with a splitting
\gr^{0}A\rightarrow F^{0}A
. Sending
F^{\geq\star}A
to the pair
(\gr^{0},\Fil^{\geq 1}A)
, we get an equivalence of this
\infty
-category and the
\infty
-category of pairs
(B,F^{\geq 1}B)
where
B
is a derived ring, and
F^{\geq 1}B
is a positively filtered non-unital derived
B
-algebra. The inverse functor associates to a pair
(B,F^{\geq 1}B)
the augmented filtered algebra
...\rightarrow F^{\geq 1}B\rightarrow B\oplus F^{\geq 1}B
. When
R
is any connective derived ring, the left hand side can be redefined as the
\infty
-category of under objects

\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{aug}}:=(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}})_{R/},

where
R
is considered as a filtered ring inserted in degree
0
10
10

10

Note that if we consider
R
as a filtered ring with constant filtration, then its associated graded ring is
0
. Therefore, any filtered ring receiving a map from
R
considered with constant filtration, would need to have associated graded ring
0
too, and therefore constant filtration is not the right approach.
.

Construction 3.2.27
.

Let
R
be a derived commutative ring.

•

Assume
R=\mathbb{Z}
. Then the
\infty
-category
\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
has a derived filtered non-unital divided power algebra
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+,\geq\star}_{\mathbb{Z}}:\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
using Construction
2.3.13
and Construction
2.4.21
. We let
\Fil\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu},\mathrm{pd}}:=\Alg_{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{+,\geq\star}}(\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}})
. The subcategory
\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}\subset\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is closed under formation of free
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{+,\geq\star}
-algebras. Therefore, there is an
\infty
-category

\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}:=\Fil\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\underset{\Fil\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}}{\times}\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}

of
derived filtered non-unital divided power
R
-algebras
, which is monadic over
\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and the monad right-left extended from the subcategory
\Ind(\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},0})
by Remark
2.4.22
.

•

If
R
is not necessarily discrete, or even connective, we can define the
\infty
-category
\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
using Construction
3.2.26
. There is a forgetful functor
\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
, and a forgetful functor
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then we define the
\infty
-category of
augmented filtered derived divided power rings
as the pull-back

\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}},}

i.e. an augmented filtered divided power ring is an augmented filtered derived ring endowed with a divided power structure on the filtered augmentation ideal. Let
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}
be the
\infty
-category of
augmented filtrations
, i.e. object
F^{\geq\star}\in\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}
endowed with a splitting of the map
F^{\geq 0}\rightarrow\gr^{0}_{F}
. We think of objects of
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}
as pairs
(X,F^{\geq 1}X)
. The
\infty
-category
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}
is monadic over
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Let
\textstyle{(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\geq\star}):\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}}
be the resulting monad. It satisfies the formula

(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\geq\star})(X,F^{\geq 1}X)\simeq(\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}(X),\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\geq\star}_{\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}(X)}(F^{\geq 1}X\otimes\mathrm{LSym}_{\mathbb{Z}}(X))).

By definition, the fiber of the forgetful functor
\ev^{0}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
over some
A
is the
\infty
-category of
non-unital filtered derived divided power
A
-algebras
11
11

11

Compare this with Definition
3.2.9
.
. We denote
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}^{\geq\star}:\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
he free algebra monad. Similarly, if
R
is a connective derived ring, we can consider
R
as a filtered derived ring inserted in degree
0
, and let

\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}:=\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\underset{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{aug}}}{\times}\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{aug}}.

Similarly, we have an
\infty
-category of
graded derived divided power rings

\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
whose fiber over any

is the
\infty
-category of
non-unital graded derived divided power
A
-algebras
, and the free algebra monad is denoted as
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+,\bullet}_{A}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 1}\mathrm{Mod}_{A}.

For future purposes, we specifically fix the following notation.

Notation 3.2.28
.

Let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
be the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,M)
, where
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
and
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
. The functor of
free graded divided power algebra on a module
is the functor:

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\bullet}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

which sends a pair
(A,M)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
to
(A,\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+,\bullet}_{A}(M))

The right adjoint of this functor is the functor

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\{0,1\}}\circ\>\mathrm{forget}_{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}.}

In the same vein, we define a
free graded derived algebra on a module

\mathrm{LSym}^{\bullet}_{A}(M)
for any pair
(A,M)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
.

Example 3.2.29
.

The free filtered non-unital divided power algebra
\Gamma^{\geq 1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(M)
on a finitely gerated free
\mathbb{Z}
-module
M
(put in filtered weight
1
) has filtration with stages

\Gamma_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq n}(M):=\bigoplus_{i=n}^{\infty}\Gamma^{i}_{\mathbb{Z}}(M),

where
n\geq 1
. Concretely, the
n
-th stage of filtration consists of elements of the form:

\Gamma^{\geq n}_{\mathbb{Z}}(M)=\{\sum_{i_{1}+...i_{k}\geq n}\gamma^{i_{1}}(x_{1})...\gamma^{i_{k}}(x_{k})|x_{1},...,x_{k}\in M,k\geq 1\}.

Construction 3.2.30
.

We define a functor
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{aug}}
by sending an object
F^{\geq\star}A
to the augmented filtered derived algebra
...\rightarrow F^{\geq 1}A\rightarrow F^{\geq 0}A\oplus F^{\geq 1}A
so that the map
F^{\geq 0}A\oplus F^{\geq 1}A\rightarrow F^{\geq 0}A
has a (zero) section
12
12

12

This is analogous to the functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}):\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{aug}}_{R}
which sends a Smith ideal
(I\rightarrow A)
to the augmented Smith ideal
(I\rightarrow A\oplus I)
.
.

Definition 3.2.31
.

We define an
\infty
-category
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
of
filtered derived divided power (pd) algebras
as the fiber square

\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{R},}

where the lower horizontal functor is the forgetful functor, and the right vertical functor is as defined in Construction
3.2.30
.

Remark 3.2.32
.

Before the next construction, we make a trivial observation. The functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}_{R}
constructed in Construction
3.1.24
lifts to a functor

\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{aug}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{aug}}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}

as for any object
F^{\geq\star}A\in\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{aug}}
, the Smith ideal
F^{\geq 1}A\rightarrow F^{0}A
has a splitting.

Construction 3.2.33
.

We will construct a functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
for any connective derived commutative ring
R
, lifting the functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}
provided by Remark
3.2.32
. This is sufficient to do in the case
A=\mathbb{Z}
. In this case, the derived divided power algebra monad is right-left extended. We observe that there is a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Ind(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z},0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\geq\star})}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},0}^{\{0,1\},\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathrm{LSym},\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{+})}
\textstyle{\Ind(\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}^{\mathrm{aug}}_{\mathbb{Z},0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\Ind(\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z},0}^{\{0,1\},\vee})}

Therefore, by right-left extension we obtain a functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{aug}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee,\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

For a commutative algebra
A
with a divided power ideal
I\subset A
, we can construct the filtration by ideals
...\subset I^{[3]}\subset I^{[2]}\subset I\subset A
where
I^{[n]}
is spanned by elements
x_{1}^{[i_{1}]}...x_{n}^{[i_{n}]}
with
i_{1}+...+i_{n}\geq n
. The associated graded algebra for this filtration is the free graded divided power algebra
\Gamma_{A/I}(I/I^{2})=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}I^{[n]}/I^{[n+1]}
.

Construction 3.2.34
.

The functor
\ev^{[0,1]}
lifts to a limit preserving functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
on the
\infty
-categories of derived divided power algebras. For that, using Definitions
3.2.31
and
3.2.12
, we need to show that the functor
\ev^{1}:\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{A}
lifts to a functor
\ev^{1}:\Fil^{\geq 1}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd},\mathrm{nu}}_{A}
naturally for any
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
, which is done in Construction
3.2.33
. We let the functor of
derived divided power (pd) adic filtration

\textstyle{\mathrm{adic}_{\mathrm{pd}}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}}

to be left adjoint of
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
.

Similarly, there is a functor
\ev^{[0,1]}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
and its left adjoint
derived adic filtration
functor
\mathrm{adic}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
.

In the next section we will compute associated gradeds of the (pd) adic filtration. We will also identify the first truncation of the (pd) adic filtraton with the universal square-zero extension.

3.3
Two-step filtrations and square-zero extensions.

In this subsection we will develop the formalism of square-zero extensions from the point of view of two-step filtrations. Our motivation for the constructions of this subsection comes from the following situation. Let
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}
be the derived De Rham cohomology of
A
over
R
. We can truncate it by filtered pieces of weights
\geq 2
to get a
“derived square-zero extension”
of
A
by the shift of the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]
. It is expectable that this square-zero extension is controlled by the
universal derivation
. The formalism developed in this subsection gives a simple proof of this fact which has the advantage of being simple to generalize to other settings.

Definition 3.3.1
.

Let
\{0,1\}
be the subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{\mathrm{ds}}
consisting of two objects
0,1\in\mathbb{Z}
, considered as a discrete category. Similarly, let
(0<1)
be the full subcategory of
\mathbb{Z}_{\leq*}
spanned by these two objects. Fix a connective derived commutative ring
R
.

•

We define the
\infty
-category of
two-step graded
objects as follows:
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{R}):=\Fun(\{0,1\}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
. An object of this
\infty
-category is the same as a pair of objects
(X^{0},X^{1})
. This
\infty
-category is a localization of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. We let
(-)^{\{0,1\}}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to be the localization functor. It sends a positively graded object
\{X^{i}\}_{i\geq 0}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to the two-step graded object
(X^{0},X^{1})\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. The
\infty
-category
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has a symmetric monoidal structure such that the localization functor is symmetric monoidal.

•

Similarly, we define the
\infty
-category of
two-step filtrations

\Fil^{[0,1]}(\mathrm{Mod}_{R}):=\Fun((0<1)^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Mod}_{R})
. An object of this
\infty
-category is the same as a map
X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0}
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. As with two-step graded objects, this
\infty
-category is a localization of
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. The localization functor
(-)^{\leq 1}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is given by the formula

(...\rightarrow X^{2}\rightarrow X^{1}\rightarrow X^{0})^{\leq 1}\simeq(X^{1}/X^{2}\rightarrow X^{0}/X^{2}).

The
\infty
-category
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has a symmetric monoidal structure making the localization functor symmetric monoidal.

Let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
be the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,M)
with
A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
and
M\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
, and
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\subset\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the full subcategory of non-negatively filtered
R
-module spectra consisting of objects concentrated in two degrees. There is a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
such that the functor
(-)^{\{0,1\}}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, left adjoint to the inclusion, is symmetric monoidal. The following observation is due to A.Raksit, see
[
26
]
.

Proposition 3.3.2
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}.}

Remark 3.3.3
.

As the functor
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(respectively,
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
) is an inclusion, we can say that a derived commutative algebra in
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(respectively, in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
) is the same as a derived commutative algebra in
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
) whose underlying linear object is in
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
). We will use notations
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
for the
\infty
-category of
two-step filtered derived algebras
and
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
for the
\infty
-category of
two-step graded derived algebras
respectively.

We mentioned an equivalence
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
before. Let us now compute the free two-step graded derived commutative algebra
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})
on an object
M^{0}\oplus M^{1}\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. For a derived commutative ring
A
and an
A
-module, we let
A\oplus M
be the derived commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
formed by taking a trivial square zero extension of
A
by
M
put in weight 1.

Proposition 3.3.4
.

There is an equivalence of derived commutative rings in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
:

\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})\simeq\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(M^{0})\oplus M^{1}.

Proof.

Indeed, let
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\bullet}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})
be the free graded derived commutative ring on
(M^{0},M^{1})
considered as an object of
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. There is a map of derived commutative algebras
\mathrm{LSym}^{\bullet}_{R}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(M^{0})\oplus M^{1}
obtained by moding out all higher symmetric powers of
M^{1}
. Applying symmetric monoidal truncation functor
(-)^{\leq 1}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, we obtain a map
\mathrm{LSym}_{R}^{\{0,1\}}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})\simeq\mathrm{LSym}^{\bullet}_{R}(M^{0}\oplus M^{1})^{\leq 1}\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{R}(M^{0})\oplus M^{1}
. By definition, truncation functor kills all pieces in weights
\geq 2
. Since
n
-th derived symmetric power of an object in weight
1
is in weight
n
, it follows that truncation of a free derived commutative algebra on an object
M^{0}\oplus M^{1}
kills all higher symmetric powers of
M^{1}
of degree
n\geq 2
. Therefore, the constructed map is an equivalence.
∎

The following remark enables us to define a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-module objects in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}.

Construction 3.3.5
.

The algebra
\mathbb{D}_{-}
has the structure of a
derived cocommutative bialgebra
in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. To see this, take the free graded associative algebra
\mathrm{T}(R(1)[-1])
on the shift of the object
R(1)[-1]\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. It has a structure of a derived cocommutative bialgebra in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Applying symmetric monoidal truncation functor
(-)^{\leq 1}
, wee see that the trivial square zero extension algebra
\mathrm{T}(R(1)[-1])^{\leq 1}\simeq R\oplus R(1)[-1]\simeq\mathbb{D}_{-}
gets the structure of a derived cocommutative bialgebra in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Definition 3.3.6
.

Using Construction
3.3.5
, we define an
\infty
-category of
differential two-step graded modules

\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}:=\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathbb{D}_{-}}(\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

as
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-modules in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
with the symmetric monoidal structure coming from the cocommutative bialgebra structure on

\mathbb{D}_{-}
. In particular, the forgetful functor
\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is symmetric monoidal. We will also consider the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}:=\mathrm{DAlg}(\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R})

of derived commutative algebra objects in this symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category, which is the same as derived differential graded algebras concentrated in degrees
0,1
. These are referred to as
derived differential two-step graded algebras in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

The filtered cousine of differential two-step graded algebras is the
\infty
-category
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
of non-negatively filtered derived algebras which are concentrated in degrees
0,1
. We can also understand them as derived commutative algebras in the symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(with the symmetric monoidal structure making truncation functor
(-)^{\leq 1}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
symmetric monoidal. Note that the functor
\gr:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
restricts to a symmetric monoidal functor
\gr:\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Proposition 3.3.7
.

The functor

\textstyle{\gr:\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}

is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

Indeed, this follows from the fact that
\gr:\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}
is an equivalence of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories, and the fact that any two-step filtration is automatically complete.
∎

Corollary 3.3.8
.

The functor
\gr
lifts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\gr:\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}.}

The category of discrete two-step graded commutative algebras
\Fil^{[0,1]}\CAlg_{R,\heartsuit}
can be seen to be equivalent to the full subcategory of the category of algebras with a Smith ideal spanned by objects
I\rightarrow A
for which the multiplication map
I\otimes_{A}I\rightarrow I
is zero. In other words, this is the category of
“generalized square-zero extensions”
. It is a classical fact in derived algebraic geometry that square-zero extensions are controlled by derivations. The equivalence of Corollary
3.3.8
suggests that the right hand side should have a description in terms of derivations, and the left hand-side in terms of derived square-zero Smith ideals. Below we will show that this is indeed the case.

In what follows, we fix a connective derived commutative ring
R
. Recall from Construction
3.1.9
that there is a lax symmetric monoidal structure on the functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}):\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
. We observe the following proposition.

Construction 3.3.9
.

The tautological functor
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
has a lax symmetric monoidal structure. We can realize it as a composition of two lax symmetric monoidal functors. First, we use lax symmetric monoidal inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Next, we consider the functor
\ev^{\{0,1\}}:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}
defined as a composition of lax symmetric monoidal functors

\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{\{0,1\}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{split}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}

The composition of the inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
with
\ev^{\{0,1\}}
thus has a lax symmetric monoidal structure.

Construction 3.3.10
.

We will construct a commutative square in the
\infty
-category
\CAlg(\Pr^{L})_{\mathrm{lax}}
of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories and lax symmetric monoidal functors

\textstyle{\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{\{0,1\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}.}

(3.7)

The upper horizontal functor
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}
is defined as the lax symmetric monoidal composition

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 60.75536pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-60.75536pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\hookrightarrow\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.15051pt\raise 6.68947pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.75536pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 84.75536pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The left vertical functor is obtained by restricting the forgetful functor
(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}):\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to two-step filtered objects. By construction, the square
3.7
fits into the larger commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{[0,1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\{0,1\}}}

where both the left and the right halves are commutative diagrams of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories and lax symmetric monoidal functors. Therefore, the initial square diagram is a commutative diagram of lax symmetric monoidal functors.

Proposition 3.3.11
.

The commutative diagram
3.7
is a pull-back square in
\CAlg(\Pr^{L})_{\mathrm{lax}}
.

Proof.

Indeed, by Proposition
2.1.2
this can be checked on the level of underlying
\infty
-categories. Since the lower horizontal functor and the upper horizontal functor are both equivalences, it follows that the diagram is a pull-back diagram.
∎

Observation 3.3.12
.

Assume
R
is a discrete ring. Then the
\infty
-categories appearing in the diagram
3.7
have an action of
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
and all the functors are lax
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}
-linear. Indeed, in the diagram
3.7
all functors are
t
-exact, and the whole diagram is derived from the diagram

\textstyle{\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{\{0,1\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R,0}^{\{0,1\},\vee}}

of small additive categories. In the latter diagram all functors are lax symmetric monoidal, and are lax equivariant with respect to the
\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}_{0}
-action. Then the desired observation follows from Example
2.3.17
by applying the functor
\mathrm{Mod}:\mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{add},\mathrm{poly}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{\St,\Sigma}
. To summarize, the diagram
3.7
is a commutative diagram in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}}(\mathrm{Cat})_{\mathrm{lax}}
, and the same argument as in Proposition
3.3.11
shows that this in fact a pull-back square in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{SSeq}^{\mathrm{gen}}}(\mathrm{Cat})_{\mathrm{lax}}
.

The following proposition follows from the previous remark and Proposition
2.1.11
.

Proposition 3.3.13
.

Let
R
be a discrete ring. The commutative square

\textstyle{\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
