\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}}

induced by the commutative square
3.7
, is a pull-back of
\infty
-categories.

Remark 3.3.14
.

Let
R
be a connective derived ring. Then we have
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\simeq(\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}})_{R/}
, where
R
is considered as a two-step filtered derived algebra by inserting it in degree
0
. Consequently, the pull-back diagram of Proposition
3.3.13
holds true in this case as well.

Construction 3.3.15
.

Let
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}:\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
be the non-unital derived symmetric algebra functor. It has the structure of a monad such that the
\infty
-category of
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}
-algebras is equivalent to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{R}
of non-unital derived commutative algebras in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. There is an augmentation map
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}\rightarrow\Id
which kills all pieces of
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}
in degrees greater than
1
. This map gives rise to an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\{0,1\},\vee}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod},}
\scriptstyle{\triv}

where the right adjoint is the
trivial algebra functor
and the left adjoint is the functor of taking the
cotangent fiber
. For an
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}
-algebra
A
, the data of descending
A
to an
\Id
-algebra along the map
\mathrm{LSym}^{\geq 1}\rightarrow\Id
will be called a
trivialization data
on
A
.

Definition 3.3.16
.

Let
R
be a connective derived commutative ring. We define the
\infty
-category of
square-zero Smith ideals
as the pull-back

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{sqzero}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Fil\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\triv}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\{0,1\},\vee}.}

Proposition 3.3.17
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{sqzero}}_{R}.

Proof.

Indeed, in the case of
R
being discrete, it follows from Proposition
3.3.11
and Proposition
3.3.13
. Unwinding the definitions, we see that this recovers the pull-back square of Definition
3.3.16
. If
R
is not discrete, see Remark
3.3.14
.
∎

To unravel an algebra-geometric description of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
, we begin with some remark concerning the underlying stable
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
for a connective derived ring
R
.

Remark 3.3.18
.

The free associative algebra in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is given by the formula
\mathrm{T}^{\{0,1\}}(X)\simeq\mathrm{T}(X)^{\leq 1}
, we can interpret
\mathbb{D}_{-}
as the
free associative algebra
in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
on the object
R[-1](1)\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Consequently, an object
M\in\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is the same as an object
M\in\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
together with a map
M(1)[-1]\simeq M\otimes R[-1](1)\rightarrow M
in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
(with square-zero condition being futile for degree reasons). By duality, the latter is equivalent to giving a map
M\rightarrow M\otimes R[1](-1)\simeq M(-1)[1]
. If
M
has graded components
M^{0},M^{1}
, then computing the tensor product in
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
wee see that
M(-1)[1]
is equivalent to the object
M^{1}[1]
put in weight
0
. This is in turn equivalent to giving a splitting
M^{0}\rightarrow M^{0}\oplus M^{1}[1]
of the projection
M^{0}\oplus M^{1}[1]\rightarrow M^{0}
. Therefore, we have the following computation of the mapping space as the fiber product

\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(M,M(-1)[1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(M^{0},M^{0}\oplus M^{1}[1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{\Id_{M^{0}}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}(M^{0},M^{0}).}

Construction 3.3.19
.

Consider the functor
\oplus[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
obtained by composing the inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
with the shear functor
[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
followed by the direct sum totalisaion
\oplus:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. In other words, the functor
\oplus[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
sends
(X^{0},X^{1})
to
X^{0}\oplus X^{1}[1]
. The lemma below show that this functor is lax symmetric monoidal. The observation of Remark
3.3.18
implies that
\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
sits in pull-back of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LEq}\bigl(\ev^{0},\oplus[\bullet])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Mod}_{R}^{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{N}},}

(3.8)

where
\mathrm{LEq}\bigl(\ev^{0},\oplus[\bullet])
is the
\infty
-category of two-step graded objects
(X^{0},X^{1})
endowed with a map
\delta:X^{0}\rightarrow X^{0}\oplus X^{1}[1]
, the vertical functor sends
(X^{0},X^{1},\delta)
to
X^{0}
endowed with the composite map
X^{0}\rightarrow X^{0}\oplus X^{1}[1]\rightarrow X^{0}
, and the horizontal functors considers any object of
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
with identity map.

The lax equalizer in the upper right corner of diagram
3.8
has a symmetric monoidal structure by Proposition
2.1.3
, and all functors involved in the diagram are symmetric monoidal. Consequently, the pull-back diagram
3.8
is a pull-back of symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories.

Lemma 3.3.20
.

The functor
\oplus[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has a lax symmetric monoidal structure.

Proof.

The functor
\oplus:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is a composition of the lax symmetric monoidal inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
followed by the direct sum totalisaion functor
\oplus:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. The latter has a symmetric monoidal structure as we can identify it with the forgetful functor from the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-representations in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
to
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Therefore, the composition has a lax symmetric monoidal structure.

It remains to check that the shear
[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
has a symmetric monoidal structure. Recall from Construction
2.4.8
that the shear on all non-negatively graded modules
[\bullet]:\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is lax symmetric monoidal with respect to Koszul sign rule symmetric monoidal structure on the target. But the symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
induced from the Koszul sign rule symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
is equivalent to the one induced by the usual symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, which is seen in the initial case
R=\mathbb{Z}
by identifying the tensor product in the heart of the negative
t
-structure.
∎

Remark 3.3.21
.

Let
R
be a connective derived ring. Using Construction
3.3.19
and Proposition
2.1.2
, and the same logic as in Proposition
3.3.13
and Remark
3.3.14
, it follows that
\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
is a pull-back of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LEq}(\ev^{0},\oplus[\bullet]:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{N}}_{R}.}

(3.9)

Moreover, we have that the functor
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus[\bullet]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}}
is equivalent to the functor sending
(A,M)
to the trivial square-zero extension
A\oplus M[1]
. The implication of the pull-back diagram
3.9
is that the data of an object
A^{\bullet}\in\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
is the same as the data of an object
(A,M)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
together with a derived algebra map
A\rightarrow A\oplus M[1]
splitting the projection.

We will now define an appropriate
\infty
-category of derived algebras endowed with a derivation which gives an equivalent description of the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Construction 3.3.22
.

Let
A
be a derived commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
, and
M
an
A
-module in
\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. A
derivation of
A
with values in
M[1]
is the data of a map
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\rightarrow M[1]
, where
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
is the cotangent complex of
A
over
R
. Let
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}}
be the
\infty
-category of pairs
(A,f:M\rightarrow N)
, where
M,N\in\mathrm{Mod}_{A}
and
f:M\rightarrow N
is a map. Let
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta{1}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
be the functor sending
A,f:M\rightarrow N)
to the pair
(A,M)
, and
\mathbb{L}[-1]:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
be the functor sending
A
to
(A,\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1])
. The
\infty
-category of (shifted) derivations
is defined as the pull-back

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}[-1]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}.}

Remark 3.3.23
.

Equivalently, a derivation of
A
with values in
M[1]
is a map of derived commutative
R
-algebras
\delta:A\rightarrow A\oplus M[1]
which splits the projection map
A\oplus M[1]\rightarrow A
. The equivalence of this two notions of derivation follows from the natural equivalence of mapping spaces

\Map_{\mathrm{Mod}_{A}}(\mathbb{L}_{A},M[1])\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R//A}}(A,A\oplus M[1]).

Remark
3.3.23
and Construction
3.9
imply the following proposition.

Proposition 3.3.24
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}.}

The importance of Proposition
3.3.24
stems from the following construction.

Construction 3.3.25
.

There is an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}[-1]}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]},}
\scriptstyle{A\leftarrow(A,M,d)}

where the left adjoint sends a derived commutative algebra
A
to the universal derivation
(A,\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1],\Id:\mathbb{L}_{A}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A})
.

Using the equivalence
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}\simeq\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
, we obtain that the left adjoint of the functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
sends a derived commutative algebra
A
to the object
A\oplus\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]
endowed with
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action coming from the universal derivation
d:A\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
.

Summarizing all the results we obtained in this section, we arrive at the following theorem.

Theorem 3.3.26
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\textstyle{\gr:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{sqzero}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]},}

such that the underlying
\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
-object of
\gr(I\rightarrow A)
is the triple
(A/I,I,\delta)
, where
\delta:A/I\rightarrow I[1]
is the boundary map for the fiber sequence
I\rightarrow A\rightarrow A/I
.

Proof.

This follows from Propositions
3.3.8
,
3.3.17
and
3.3.24
.
∎

Remark 3.3.27
.

Let us give a more explicit description of the functors involved in the equivalence of Theorem
3.3.26
. Let
(B,M,\delta)\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}
. We define a new derived commutative algebra with a map to
B
as a pull-back

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Id,\delta)}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Id,0)}
\textstyle{B\oplus M[1].}

We have an equivalence
\fib(A\rightarrow B)\simeq M
, moreover this is an equivalence of non-unital
A
-algebras, where
M
is endowed with trivial non-unital algebra structure. Therefore, we can consider
\{...\rightarrow 0\rightarrow M\rightarrow A\}
as a filtered objects concentrated in degrees
[0,1]
.

The inverse functor
\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}
can be described as follows. Assume
(I\rightarrow A)\in\Fil^{[0,1]}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}.
The left
A
-module structure on
I
canonically descends to an
A/I
-module structure as all multiplication maps
I^{\otimes^{n}}\rightarrow I
are naturally null-homotopic for degree reasons. Consider the tensor product
A/I\underset{A}{\otimes}A/I
. The unit
A/I\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}_{A/I}(I)
induces a map

\textstyle{A/I\underset{A}{\otimes}A/I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/I\underset{\mathrm{LSym}_{A/I}(I)}{\otimes}A/I\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{LSym}_{A/I}(I[1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A/I\oplus I[1],}

which defines two different
A/I
-algebra structures on
A/I\oplus I[1]
, coming from the left and the right action of
A/I
on
A/I\underset{A}{\otimes}A/I
. The right action gives a non-trivial derivation
\delta:A/I\rightarrow A/I\oplus I[1]
such that the map
A/I\rightarrow I[1]
is the extension class of the two-step filtration
I\rightarrow A
.

We will now unravel some relations between the cotangent complex of the map
A\rightarrow A/I
and the Smith ideal
I\rightarrow A
.

Construction 3.3.28
.

We will also consider an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}[-1]}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod},}
\scriptstyle{\triv[1]}

where the right adjoint is the functor of
trivial square-zero extension

(A,M)\longmapsto(A\rightarrow A\oplus M[1])
, and the left adjoint sends an arrow
(A\rightarrow B)
to the pair
(B,\mathbb{L}_{B/A}[-1])\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}
. By means of the equivalence
\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}
, we obtain a functor
\textstyle{\mathbb{L}[-1]\circ\cofib:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{L}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}}
. The right adjoint of
\mathbb{L}[-1]\circ\cofib
identifies with the composite functor
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\triv[1]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\fib}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}
which sends a pair
(A,M)
to the Smith ideal

\textstyle{\bigl(\fib(A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Id,0)}
\textstyle{A\oplus M)\rightarrow A\bigr)\simeq\bigl(M[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{A\bigr),}

and this functor is equivalent to the functor

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 62.338pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-62.338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\simeq\mathrm{Gr}^{\{0,1\}}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.0455pt\raise 7.06445pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.70335pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{split}^{[0,1]}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 116.338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 116.338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This construction makes transparent the folliowing proposition:

Proposition 3.3.29
.

Let
(I\rightarrow A)
be a Smith ideal corresponding to a map
A\rightarrow B\simeq A/I
. There is an equivalence of graded derived algebras

\gr^{\bullet}\mathrm{adic}(I\rightarrow A)\simeq\mathrm{LSym}^{\bullet}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{(B/A}[-1]).

Proof.

Indeed, it follows from commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\mathrm{Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{split}^{[0,1]}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{[0,1]}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{split}}

by passing to left adjoints, and using Construction
3.3.28
.
∎

Remark 3.3.30
.

Using the same notations as before, we let Let
I^{n}:=\Fil^{n}\mathrm{adic}(I\rightarrow A)
be the
n
-th stage of the divided power adic filtration corresponding to this ideal. Then we have fiber sequences

I^{n+1}\rightarrow I^{n}\rightarrow\mathrm{LSym}^{n}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A}[-1]).

In particular, for
n=1
, we get
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\simeq\cofib(I^{2}\rightarrow I)[1]
, and we get a derivation
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\rightarrow I[1]
.

We also have an equivalence of two-step filtered objects

\mathrm{adic}(I\rightarrow A)^{\leq 1}\simeq\Bigl(\frac{I}{I^{2}}\rightarrow\frac{A}{I^{2}}\Bigr),

which implies an equivalence of graded objects

\gr^{\{0,1\}}\;\mathrm{adic}(I\rightarrow A)\simeq\Bigl(\frac{I}{I^{2}},\frac{A}{I^{2}}/\frac{I}{I^{2}}\Bigr)\simeq\Bigl(\frac{I}{I^{2}},\frac{A}{I}\Bigr),

and the
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action
A/I\rightarrow I/I^{2}[1]\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
matches with the universal derivation for the map
A\rightarrow B
.

Example 3.3.31
.

Let us specify to the case when
I\rightarrow A
is a regular ideal in a discrete algebra
A
. A standard argument shows that the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\simeq I/I^{2}[1]
, and hence is a shift of the compact projective
A/I
-module
I/I^{2}\simeq I\otimes_{A}A/I
. It follows by induction from the fiber sequences

I^{n+1}\rightarrow I^{n}\rightarrow\Sym^{n}_{A/I}(I/I^{2})

that all
A
-modules
I^{n}
are discrete, and therefore the object
\mathrm{adic}(I\rightarrow A)
identifies with the classical adic filtration of the ideal
I
. Taking divided power envelopes, we get that
\mathrm{adic}^{\mathrm{pd}}\;\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(I\rightarrow A)
is the divided power adic filtration on the divided power envelope
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(I\rightarrow A)
.

In the same way, we have:

Proposition 3.3.32
.

For any
I\rightarrow A\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}
with
B=A/I
, there is an equivalence of graded derived divided power algebras

\gr^{\bullet}\mathrm{adic}_{\mathrm{pd}}(I\rightarrow A)\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\bullet}_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{B/A}[-1]).

4
Derived De Rham complex and derived crystalline cohomology.

4.1
Divided powers and derived De Rham complex.

We consider the
\infty
-category
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
introduced in Definition
3.2.31
, and the
\infty
-category
\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}}_{R}
consisting of complete objects. We also let the
\infty
-category of
differential (non-negatively) graded derived divided power algebras

\mathrm{DG}^{\geq 0}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}
be defined as the pull-back

\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\geq 0}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\gr}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}}.}

The functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
preserves all limits, and therefore has a left adjoint. Consequently, so does the functor
\gr^{0}:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
.

Definition 4.1.1
.

We will define two notions of derived De Rham cohomology, one in terms of filtered derived divided power algebras, and another in terms of differential graded derived divided power algebras.

1.

We define the
Hodge-filtered derived De Rham cohomology

\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{-/R}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}}

as the left adjoint of the functor
\gr^{0}
. The composition with the completion functor
\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\rightarrow\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}}_{R}
is the
Hodge completed derived De Rham cohomology

\widehat{\mathrm{L\Omega}}^{\geq\star}_{-/R}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\widehat{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}}_{R}.

2.

We also define the
derived De Rham complex

\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\bullet}_{-/R}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\geq 0}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

as the composition of
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}
with the functor
\gr:\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}\rightarrow\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
. Alternatively, it is the left adjoint of the functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DG}_{-}^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}^{\mathrm{pd}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}
.

3.

Letting
R
vary, we obtain the functor of
relative Hodge-filtered derived De Rham cohomology
on the
\infty
-category of arrows

\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{-/-}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}},}

as well as its graded cousin, the
relative derived De Rham complex

\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\bullet}_{-/-}
, and the complete version
relative Hodge completed derived De Rham cohomology

\widehat{\mathrm{L\Omega}}_{-/-}^{\geq\star}
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

The composition

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\fib}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{adic}_{\mathrm{pd}}}
\textstyle{\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mathrm{pd}}}

is equivalent to
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{-/-}
. Indeed, it suffices to check that the right adjoints of both functors match. The right adjoint of this composition sends
F^{\geq\star}A
to the arrow
A\rightarrow A/F^{1}A\simeq\gr^{0}A
. But the right adjoint of
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{-/-}
also sends
F^{\ast}A\longmapsto(A\rightarrow\gr^{0}A)
, and the claim follows by uniqueness of the left adjoint.

Example 4.1.3
.

Let
A\rightarrow A/I
be a surjective arrow whose kernel is an ideal generated by a
regular
sequence
I=(t_{1},t_{2},...,t_{n})
. Then it follows from Remark
4.1.2
that the derived De Rham cohomology
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{(A/I)/A}
gets identified with the algebra
A\langle x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{n}\rangle/(x_{1}-t_{1},...,x_{n}-t_{n})
endowed with the divided power filtration.

Theorem 4.1.4
.

For any derived commutative
R
-algebra
A
, there are natural equivalences:

•

\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\bullet}_{A/R}\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\bullet}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])
of graded derived divided power algebras;

•

\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\{0,1\}}_{A/R}\simeq A\oplus\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1](1)
of differential two-step graded derived algebras, with the
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action on the latter given by the universal derivation
d:A\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
.

Proof.

Let
I=\fib(R\rightarrow A)
. The the first equivalence follows from Remark
4.1.2
and Propositions
3.3.32
:

\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}\simeq\gr\mathrm{adic}_{\mathrm{pd}}\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(R,I)\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{\bullet}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]).

For the second equivalence, consider the universal property of the object
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\{0,1\}}_{A/R}
. For a fixed
R
, the functor
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\{0,1\}}_{-/R}
is the composition of left adjoints

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\bullet}_{-/R}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\geq 0}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\{0,1\}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DG}^{\{0,1\}}_{-}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}\simeq\mathrm{DAlg}_{R}\Der^{[1]}.}

The right adjoint of the composition is the functor that forgets the derivation. Therefore, the composite left adjoint is given by the functor
\mathbb{L}_{-/R}[-1]
of Construction
3.3.25
.
∎

Corollary 4.1.5
.

Let
R\rightarrow A
be a smooth map of discrete commutative algebras. Then the derived De Rham complex
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}
is discrete in the negative
t
-structure on
\mathrm{DG}_{-}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
.

Proof.

Indeed, this follows from the décalage isomorphism of Proposition
2.4.9
:
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{n}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R})[-n]
. The cotangent complex is equivalent to the projective
A
-module of Kahler differentials
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\simeq\Omega^{1}_{A/R}
. Therefore, the derived exterior powers
\mathrm{L\Lambda}^{n}_{A}(\Omega^{1}_{A/R})
are discrete too, and the
n
-th graded piece of the De Rham complex,
\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1])\simeq\Lambda^{n}_{A}(\Omega^{1}_{A/R})[-n]
sits in homological degree
-n
.
∎

To relate our derived De Rham complex with the classical De Rham complex in the smooth case, we use the
shear equivalence
.

Proposition 4.1.6
.

Let
R\rightarrow A
be a discrete map of smooth commutative rings. Then the image
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}[\bullet]
under the shear equivalence is a discrete commutative algebra in
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R,\heartsuit}
(with respect to the Koszul sign rule symmetric monoidal structure). There is an equivalence

\Omega_{A/R}^{\bullet}[\bullet]\simeq\Lambda_{A}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{A/R}),

and the
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action on
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}
translates into the De Rham differential on
\Lambda^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega^{1}_{A/R})
.

Proof.

Indeed, by Corollary
4.1.5
, properties 1) and 2) of the shear equivalence from Construction
2.4.7
, the algebra
\Omega^{\bullet}_{A/R}[\bullet]
is discrete in the neutral
t
-structure on
\mathrm{Gr}\mathrm{Mod}_{R}
and has the structure of a graded supercommutative algebra. In weight
1
, we have
(\Omega_{A/R}^{\bullet}[\bullet])^{1}\simeq\Omega^{1}_{A/R}
. Therefore, we have a map from the free graded supercommutative algebras
\Lambda^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega_{A/R}^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}[\bullet]
. By décalage isomorphism, the map
\Lambda^{n}(\Omega^{1}_{A/R})\rightarrow\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}(\Omega_{A/R}^{1}[-1])[\bullet]
is an equivalence for any
n
(see the proof of Proposition
2.4.9
), and therefore, the map
\Lambda^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega_{A/R}^{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}^{\bullet}[\bullet]
is an equivalence. The claim about De Rham differential follows from property 3) of Construction
2.4.7
, second part of Theorem
4.1.4
saying that the
\mathbb{D}_{-}
-action on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{A/R}
is the universal derivation
d:A\rightarrow\Omega^{1}_{A/R}
in degree
1
, and the classical fact that De Rham differential on
\Lambda^{\bullet}_{A}(\Omega_{A/R}^{1})
is the unique square-zero map which restricts to the universal derivation
d:A\rightarrow\Omega_{A/R}^{1}
in degree
1
.
∎

4.2
Cosimplicial techniques.

In this subsection we will revisit some well-known cosimplicial techniques for computing derived De Rham cohomology. Similar formulas were obtained by similar methods in the paper
[
3
]
, albeit formulated in a different language. These methods will be used for the theorem of comparison of the Hodge completed derived De Rham cohomology with the Hodge completed stacky De Rham cohomology cohomology in the paper
[
21
]
.

Construction 4.2.1
.

Let
R\rightarrow A
be a map in
\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. Then the Bar construction of
A
over
R
is the cosimplicial diagram:

\textstyle{\overset{\bullet}{\underset{R}{\bigotimes}}\;A:=\bigl(A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes_{R}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{R}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{...\bigr)}

Let us apply this construction to the map
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/R}\rightarrow A.
We obtain a cosimplicial diagram

\textstyle{\overset{\bullet}{\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes}}A:=\bigl(A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes}A\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{...\bigr)}

of filtered derived
R
-algebras. Taking
\gr^{0}
, we obtain for any
n
:

\gr^{0}(\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{n}}A)\simeq\underset{\gr^{0}\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{n}}A\simeq\underset{A}{\bigotimes^{n}}\;A\simeq A,

and using commutativity of
\gr^{0}
with limits it follows that

\gr^{0}\circ\Tot(\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{\bullet}}A)\simeq\Tot\circ\gr^{0}(\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{\bullet}}A)\simeq A.

In particular, it follows that the fiber of the map
\Tot(\overset{\bullet}{\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes}}A)\rightarrow A
has a natural divided power structure. By universal property of Hodge filtered derived De Rham cohomology, we obtain a filtered divided power map

\varepsilon:\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}\rightarrow\Tot(\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{\bullet}}A)

Lemma 4.2.2
.

The map
\varepsilon
is an equivalence on completions.

Proof.

Passing to associated gradeds, it suffices to show that the map

\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\Tot(\underset{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])}{\bigotimes^{\bullet}}A)

(4.1)

arising as the Cech nerve of the map
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])\rightarrow A
is an equivalence. There is an equivalence

\underset{\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])}{\bigotimes^{n}}A\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}^{\oplus^{n}}),

and we can identify the map
4.1
with the map obtained by applying the functor
\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A/R}(-)
to the cosimplicial diagram:

\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]\simeq\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.6302pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.6302pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Tot\bigl(0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.6302pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.6302pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.6302pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.44035pt\raise-4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.44035pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.44035pt\raise 4.30554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 92.44035pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{L}_{A/R}^{\oplus^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.2505pt\raise-6.45831pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.2505pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.2505pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 145.2505pt\raise 6.45831pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 145.2505pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{...\bigr).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Consequently, to prove that the map
4.1
is a graded equivalence, it suffices to show that the map

\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}_{A/R}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])\simeq\mathrm{L\Gamma}^{n}_{A/R}\bigl(\Tot(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}^{\oplus^{\bullet}})\bigr)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\Tot\bigl(\mathrm{L\Gamma}_{A/R}^{n}(\mathbb{L}_{A/R}^{\oplus^{\bullet}})\bigr)

is an equivalence for any
n
. This follows by commutativity of the derived divided power functors with finite totalizations, see
[
7
, Proposition 3.37]
.
∎

Lemma 4.2.3
.

For any
n\geq 1
, there is an equivalence

\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}}{\bigotimes^{n}}A\simeq\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A/\bigotimes^{n}A},

where
A
is considered as algebra over the tensor product
\bigotimes^{n}A
via the multiplication map
\bigotimes^{n}A\rightarrow A
.

Proof.

Indeed, more generally for a map of the form
A\underset{R}{\otimes}B\rightarrow C
, there is a natural map

\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{C/A}\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{C/R}}{\otimes}\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\star}_{C/B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{C/A\underset{R}{\otimes}B}}

which is an equivalence. This follows from commutativity with colimits of the relative De Rham cohomology functor
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/-}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\rightarrow\Fil^{\geq 0}\mathrm{DAlg}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{R}
:

\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A\underset{C}{\otimes}B\rightarrow R}\simeq\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A\rightarrow R\underset{C\rightarrow R}{\otimes}B\rightarrow R}\simeq\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{A\rightarrow R}\underset{\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{C\rightarrow R}}{\otimes}\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\geq\star}_{B\rightarrow R}.

Applying this equivalence to the map
A\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow A
and using
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A/A}\simeq A
, we get the claim by induction.
∎

Let
R\rightarrow A
be a map of derived commutative rings, and denote

I_{n}:=\fib(A\otimes_{R}A\otimes...\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow A)

the fiber of the
n
-times multiplication map. The collection of all
I_{n}
for all
n
organize into a cosimplicial Smith ideal in the cosimplicial algerba
A^{\otimes^{\bullet}_{R}}
. Let
\widehat{\Env}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\otimes^{n}_{R}A}(I_{n})
be the completed divided power envelope of
\otimes_{R}^{n}A
at the ideal
I_{n}
. There are equivalences
\widehat{\mathrm{L\Omega}}_{A/\otimes_{R}^{n}A}\simeq\widehat{\Env}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\otimes^{n}_{R}A}(I_{n})
for each
n
, commuting with cosimplicial maps. This implies the following:

Proposition 4.2.4
.

There is an equivalence of complete filtered derived divided power algebras

\textstyle{\widehat{\mathrm{L\Omega}}^{\geq\star}_{A/R}\simeq\Tot\bigl(A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Env}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\otimes^{2}_{R}A}(I_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Env}^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\otimes^{3}_{R}A}(I_{3})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{...\bigr)}

Remark 4.2.5
.

Note that when
R\rightarrow A
is a map of connective derived commutative rings, then all the ideals
I_{n}
are connective, and hence so are the divided power envelopes
\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}_{\otimes^{2}_{R}A}(I_{n})
. The formula of Proposition
4.2.4
tells that the (non-connective general) object
\widehat{\mathrm{L\Omega}}_{A/R}^{\geq\star}
can be computed as a totalisaion of connective objects.

4.3
Derived crystalline cohomology.

As we have seen in the previous subsection, derived De Rham cohomology of
A
can be thought as the universal filtered divided power thickening of
A
. Crystalline cohomology is also a universal divided power thickening of
A
, but with one crucial difference: in crystalline cohomology theory we require compatibility of divided powers with divided powers on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. More generally, crystalline cohomology can be defined relatively to any divided power algebra
I\rightarrow A
: given a derived
A/I
-algebra
R
, there exists a universal derived divided power
(I\rightarrow A)
-algebra
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/A)
which has
R
as the zeroth graded piece. While the idea is classical, we learnt from B.Antieau a formulation of the universal property of the crystalline cohomology theory in the spirit of the paper of A.Raksit
[
26
]
.

Definition 4.3.1
.

Let
(I\rightarrow A)
be a connective derived divided power algebra, and denote
\overline{A}:=A/I
. Consider the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
of derived divided power maps
(R\rightarrow\overline{R})
under
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
. There is a functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}
sending an object
(R\rightarrow\overline{R})
to the derived
\overline{A}
-algebra
\overline{R}\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}
. This functor preserves all limits, and hence admits a right adjoint. We define
derived crystalline cohomology (relative to
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(A\rightarrow\overline{A})):\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.06947pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.06947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.06947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.06947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

as the left adjoint of
\ev^{0}
.

This definition can be given in families, i.e. letting the base
(I\rightarrow A)
vary. We define the
absolute crystalline site

\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}
as the
\infty
-category of pairs

\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}:=\{\>\>(A\rightarrow\overline{A},R)\>\>|\>(A\rightarrow\overline{A})\in\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\>\>,\>\>R\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\}.

The functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}
sending a derived divided power map
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
to the pair
(A\rightarrow\overline{A},\overline{A})
admits a left adjoint

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.3306pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.3306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.3306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.3306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

whose value on the pair
(A\rightarrow\overline{A},R)
is
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))
. When the base divided power ideal is clear from the definition, we simply denote it as
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/A)
.

Example 4.3.2
.

Let
A
be any derived ring, and consider the identity map
(\Id:A\rightarrow A)
as the base derived divided power algebra. Then the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{(A\rightarrow A)/}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}
of derived divided power maps over
A
, and the functor
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(\Id:A\rightarrow A))
identifies with the derived De Rham cohomology functor
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/A}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{A}
.

Example 4.3.3
.

Let
A=\mathbb{Z}/(p^{n})
endowed with the pd-ideal
(p)\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}/(p^{n})
so that
\overline{\mathbb{Z}/(p^{n})}=\mathbb{F}_{p}
. In this case, for a discrete
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
R
, the derived divided power algebra
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/\mathbb{Z}/(p^{n}))
is related to the (mod
p^{n}
) classical crystalline cohomology theory of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebras (the precise statement will be explained below). In particular, taking the limit as
n
goes to infinity, we obtain a
p
-complete derived divided power algebra

\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\underset{\leftarrow}{\lim}\>\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/\mathbb{Z}/(p^{n}))\in\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd},\wedge}.

This should recover the classical crystalline cohomology of
R
relative to the ring of
p
-adic integers.

Let us now discuss what properties we expect derived crystalline cohomology theory to satisfy. First, given a pd base
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
and a derived
\overline{A}
-algebra
R
, the base change
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
should be equivalent to the derived De Rham cohomology
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/\overline{A}}
.

Another property is that for a derived
\overline{A}
-algebra
\overline{R}
, whenever we have a lift
R
of
\overline{R}
to a derived
A
-algebra, we can compute the crystalline cohomology of
\overline{R}
relative to
A
in terms as the derived De Rham cohomology of
R
.

To formulate the last property, let us introduce some notation. Let
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
be a base derived divided power map, and
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))
be as in Definition
4.3.1
. Let

(\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\overline{A}):=\Env^{\mathrm{pd}}(A\rightarrow\overline{A})\simeq\mathrm{L\Omega}_{\overline{A}/A}

be the derived divided power envelope of
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
. By adjunction, the pd structure on
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
gives a natural pd map
(\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
. Denote
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/A}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}
be the derived De Rham cohomology functor of derived
\overline{A}
-algebras, taken relatively to
A
and endowed with a natural
\mathcal{A}
-linear structure. Then the base change
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/A}\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}A
should compute derived crystalline cohomology
\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(R/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))
for any derived
\overline{A}
-algebra
R
.

All these properties are almost immediate in our setting, as we will see now. The main ingredient is the following base-change property.

Proposition 4.3.4
.

Assume
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow(B\rightarrow\overline{B})
is a map of derived divided power bases. Then the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\otimes_{\overline{A}}\overline{B}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{(A\rightarrow\overline{A})/}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\otimes_{(A\rightarrow\overline{A})}(B\rightarrow\overline{B})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(B\rightarrow\overline{B}))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{(B\rightarrow\overline{B})/}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}.}

Proof.

Follows by passing to right adjoints.
∎

The following statement follows from the base-change property and summarizes the main computational properties of derived crystalline cohomology.

Corollary 4.3.5
.

Assume
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
is a base derived divided power algebra,
I=\fib(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
and
(\mathcal{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{A})
as defined above.

1.

The composite functor

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{(A\rightarrow\overline{A})/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\otimes_{A}\overline{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{\overline{A}}}

is equivalent to derived De Rham cohomology
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/\overline{A}}
relative to
\overline{A}
.

2.

There is a commutative diagram of functors

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/A}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/A)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{-\otimes_{\mathcal{A}}A}

3.

Consider the map
(0\rightarrow A)\rightarrow(I\rightarrow A)
of derived divided power algebras. There is a commutative triangle of functors

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/A}}
\scriptstyle{-\underset{A}{\otimes}\overline{A}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\overline{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{(\Id:A\rightarrow A)/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\underset{(0\rightarrow A)}{\otimes}(A\rightarrow\overline{A})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{(A\rightarrow\overline{A})/}.}

Proof.

For the first, apply Proposition
4.3.4
and Example
4.3.2
for the map
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow(\Id:\overline{A}\rightarrow\overline{A})
. For the second, use the map
(\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\overline{A})\rightarrow(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
. For the third, the map
(0\rightarrow A)\rightarrow(I\rightarrow A)
.
∎

Remark 4.3.6
.

Let us unwind the third statement of Corollary. Let
R
be a derived
A
-algebra and
\overline{R}:=R\otimes_{A}\overline{A}
. Consider the derived De Rham complex of
R
taken relatively to
A
,
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/A}
. This has the structure of a derived divided power algebra whose zeroth graded piece is
R
. Now endow
\mathrm{L\Omega}^{\wedge}_{R/A}
with a different derived pd ideal defined as the fiber of the composite map

\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/A}\rightarrow R\rightarrow\overline{R}.

This gives a derived divided power
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
-algebra structure on
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/A}
, and the assignment
R\longmapsto\mathrm{L\Omega}_{R/A}
determines a functor
\mathrm{L\Omega}_{-/A}:\mathrm{DAlg}_{A}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd}}_{(A\rightarrow\overline{A})}
. Then the statement is that there is an equivalence of derived divided power
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
-algebras

\Omega_{R/A}\simeq\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\overline{R}/(A\rightarrow\overline{A})).

Example 4.3.7
.

Let
\overline{A}[x]
be the polynomial algebra on one generator over
\overline{A}
. It has a essentially unique lift
A[x]
over
A
. It follows from the third part of Corollary
4.3.5
that the crystalline cohomology of
\overline{A}[x]
is equivalent to the De Rham cohomology of
A[x]
:

\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(\overline{A}[x]/(A\rightarrow\overline{A}))\simeq\mathrm{L\Omega}_{A[x]/A}.

Remark 4.3.8
.

Let
\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}_{\geq 0}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}
be the full subcategory consisting of pairs
(A\rightarrow\overline{A},R)
where all derived rings are connective. Using Proposition
3.2.25
, this
\infty
-category is equivalent to the
derived crystalline context
, the notion defined in the paper
[
22
]
. In particular, it is equivalent to the sifted completion of a small category
\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}},\mathrm{poly}}
consisting of pairs
(A\rightarrow\overline{A},R)
where the map
(A\rightarrow\overline{A})
has the form
\mathbb{Z}\langle x_{1},...,x_{n}\rangle\otimes\mathbb{Z}[y_{1},...,y_{m}]\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[y_{1},...,y_{m}]
and
R
is a polynomial
\overline{A}
-algebra. Zhouhang Mao defined derived crystalline cohomology as a functor
\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}}}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
13
13

13

to be more precise, he defines a functor with values in
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, but it is clear from the construction that it lifts to a functor with values in derived rings
as the left Kan extension of the classical crystalline cohomology functor
\mathbb{Z}^{{\mathrm{crys}},\mathrm{poly}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. The resulting functor commutes with all colimits. As the classical crystalline cohomology of polynomial algebras can be computed as the De Rham cohomology of a essentially unique lift, it follows from Example
4.3.7
that our approach to derived crystalline cohomology recovers the approach of Zhouhang Mao.

In the rest of this subsection, we treat in more details the particular case of the pd base
(p)\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. In particular, we will explain how to compute the crystalline cohomology using a lift to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and relate derived crystalline cohomology relative to
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with the formula for the De Rham stack in characteristic
p
.

By abuse of notation, let us denote
\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(-/\mathbb{Z}_{p}):\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
the functor sending a derived commutative
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
A
to its
p
-completed derived crystalline cohomology understood merely as a derived commutative
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra. In other words, this is the composition

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}^{\wedge}(-/\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\Delta^{1},\mathrm{pd},\wedge}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ev^{0}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\wedge}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}.}

Below we will explain how to compute the right adjoint of this functor.

Definition 4.3.9
.

Recall that the right adjoint of the forgetful functor
\ev^{0}:\mathrm{DAlg}^{\Delta^{1}_{\vee},\mathrm{pd}}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{Z}}
is the functor sending a derived commutative ring
R
to the derived divided power Smith ideal
(R^{\sharp}\rightarrow R)
, where
R^{\sharp}
is the pd radical of
R
. For any derived commutative algebra
R
, we define the
pd-reduction
of
R
by the formula
R_{\mathrm{pdred}}:=R/R^{\sharp}
.

Proposition 4.3.10
.

There is an adjunction

\textstyle{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{DAlg}^{\wedge}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{(-)_{\mathrm{pdred}}}

Remark 4.3.11
.

Let
\Spec(\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p})):\mathrm{DAlg}^{\wedge}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\geq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spc}
be the spectrum of the non-connective derived ring
\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
. Then the functor of points formula for
\Spec(\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}))
is

\Spec(\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}))(R)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}^{\wedge}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(\mathrm{R}\Gamma_{{\mathrm{crys}}}^{\wedge}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}),R)\simeq\Map_{\mathrm{DAlg}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}}(A,R_{\mathrm{pdred}}).

It follows that
\Spec(\mathrm{R}\Gamma^{\wedge}_{{\mathrm{crys}}}(A/\mathbb{Z}_{p}))\simeq X_{{\mathrm{crys}}},
where crystallization of
X=\Spec A
is as defined in
[
2
]
and
[
9
]
.
